To estimate
\big|(\frac{\partial V_{i}^{(1)}}{\partial u})(\rho,u)-(\frac{\partial V_{i}^{(0)}}{\partial u})(\rho,u)\big|
,
we divide it into six cases:

Case 1.
\rho_{i}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{*}
: It follows from (
4.13
)–(
4.14
)
and (
4.1
)–(
4.34
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(1)}-V_{i}^{(0)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho}\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(s)}{2k^{\prime}(s)}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\Big|\Big(\frac{\partial V_{j}^{(0)}}{\partial u}\Big)(s,u^{(i)}(s))\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{i})}{2k^{\prime}(\rho_{i})}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\big|V_{j}^{(0)}(\rho_{i},u_{i})\big|\,\Big|\frac{\partial\rho_{i}}{\partial u}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho}\frac{\nu}{2s}\,(2\bar{C}_{2}s)\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\nu}{2\rho_{i}}\,(2C_{1}\rho_{i}^{1+\theta_{1}})\,(C_{2}\rho_{i}^{1-\theta_{1}})

\displaystyle=\bar{C}_{2}\nu\rho-\bar{C}_{2}\nu\rho_{1}+C_{1}C_{2}\nu\rho_{i}\leq\bar{C}_{2}\nu\rho,

(4.35)

where, in the last inequality of (
4.1
), we have chosen

\bar{C}_{2}\geq C_{1}C_{2}.

(4.36)

Case 2.
\rho_{i}\leq\rho_{*}\leq\rho\leq\rho^{*}
: Then, similarly, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(1)}-V_{i}^{(0)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho_{*}}+\int_{\rho_{*}}^{\rho}\Big)\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(s)}{2k^{\prime}(s)}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\Big|\Big(\frac{\partial V_{j}^{(0)}}{\partial u}\Big)(s,u^{(i)}(s))\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{i})}{2k^{\prime}(\rho_{i})}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\big|V_{j}^{(0)}(\rho_{i},u_{i})\big|\,\Big|\frac{\partial\rho_{i}}{\partial u}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{2}\big(\rho^{1+M_{2}}-(\rho_{*})^{1+M_{2}}\big)\varpi_{M_{2}}+\bar{C}_{2}\nu(\rho_{*}-\rho_{i})+C_{1}C_{2}\nu\rho_{i}\leq\tilde{C}_{2}\rho\,\varpi_{M_{2}},

(4.37)

where
\varpi_{M_{2}}:=\frac{C_{0}}{1+M_{2}}

and,
in the last inequality of (
4.1
), we have used (
4.36
) and chosen

\tilde{C}_{2}\geq\bar{C}_{2}(\rho_{*})^{-M_{2}}\,\nu\varpi_{M_{2}}^{-1}.

(4.38)

Case 3.
\rho_{*}\leq\rho_{i}\leq\rho\leq\rho^{*}
: It follows that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(1)}-V_{i}^{(0)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho}\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(s)}{2k^{\prime}(s)}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\Big|\Big(\frac{\partial V_{j}^{(0)}}{\partial u}\Big)(s,u^{(i)}(s))\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s+\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{i})}{2k^{\prime}(\rho_{i})}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\big|V_{j}^{(0)}(\rho_{i},u_{i})\big|\,\Big|\frac{\partial\rho_{i}}{\partial u}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{2}\big(\rho^{1+M_{2}}-\rho_{i}^{1+M_{2}}\big)\varpi_{M_{2}}+\tilde{C_{1}}C_{2}C_{0}\rho_{i}^{1+M_{1}-\theta_{2}}\leq\tilde{C}_{2}\rho\,\varpi_{M_{2}},

(4.39)

where, in the last inequality of (
4.1
), we have chosen

M_{1}\geq M_{2}+\theta_{2},\qquad\tilde{C}_{2}\geq\tilde{C}_{1}C_{2}(1+M_{2})(\rho^{*})^{M_{1}-M_{2}-\theta_{2}}.

(4.40)

Case 4.
\rho_{i}\leq\rho_{*}<\rho^{*}\leq\rho
: For this case, similarly, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(1)}-V_{i}^{(0)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho_{*}}+\int_{\rho_{*}}^{\rho^{*}}+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}\Big)\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(s)}{2k^{\prime}(s)}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\Big|\Big(\frac{\partial V_{j}^{(0)}}{\partial u}\Big)(s,u^{(i)}(s))\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{i})}{2k^{\prime}(\rho_{i})}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\big|V_{j}^{(0)}(\rho_{i},u_{i})\big|\,\Big|\frac{\partial\rho_{i}}{\partial u}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}_{2}(\rho-\rho^{*})\nu+\tilde{C}_{2}\big((\rho^{*})^{1+M_{2}}-(\rho_{*})^{1+M_{2}}\big)\varpi_{M_{2}}+\bar{C}_{2}(\rho_{*}-\rho_{i})\nu+C_{1}C_{2}\rho_{i}\nu

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu,

(4.41)

where, in the last inequality of (
4.1
), we have used (
4.36
) and (
4.38
) and chosen

\hat{C}_{2}\geq\tilde{C}_{2}(\rho^{*})^{M_{2}}\,\nu^{-1}\varpi_{M_{2}}.

(4.42)

Case 5.
\rho_{*}\leq\rho_{i}\leq\rho^{*}\leq\rho
: Then

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(1)}-V_{i}^{(0)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho^{*}}+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}\Big)\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(s)}{2k^{\prime}(s)}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\Big|\Big(\frac{\partial V_{j}^{(0)}}{\partial u}\Big)(s,u^{(i)}(s))\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{i})}{2k^{\prime}(\rho_{i})}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\big|V_{j}^{(0)}(\rho_{i},u_{i})\big|\,\Big|\frac{\partial\rho_{i}}{\partial u}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}_{2}(\rho-\rho^{*})\nu+\tilde{C}_{2}\big((\rho^{*})^{1+M_{2}}-\rho_{i}^{1+M_{2}}\big)\varpi_{M_{2}}+\tilde{C}_{1}C_{2}C_{0}\rho_{i}^{1+M_{1}-\theta_{2}}\leq\hat{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu,

(4.43)

where we have used (
4.40
) and (
4.42
) in the last inequality of (
4.1
).

Case 6.
\rho^{*}\leq\rho_{i}\leq\rho
: It follows similarly that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(1)}-V_{i}^{(0)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho}\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(s)}{2k^{\prime}(s)}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\Big|\Big(\frac{\partial V_{j}^{(0)}}{\partial u}\Big)(s,u^{(i)}(s))\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s+\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{i})}{2k^{\prime}(\rho_{i})}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\big|V_{j}^{(0)}(\rho_{i},u_{i})\big|\,\Big|\frac{\partial\rho_{i}}{\partial u}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}_{2}\rho\nu-\hat{C}_{2}\rho_{i}\nu+\hat{C}_{1}C_{2}\rho_{i}\nu\leq\hat{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu,

(4.44)

where, in the last inequality of (
4.1
), we have chosen

\hat{C}_{2}\geq\hat{C}_{1}C_{2}.

(4.45)

Combining (
4.1
)–(
4.45
), we conclude that, for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(1)}-V_{i}^{(0)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|\leq\begin{cases}\bar{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu\quad&\text{for }\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
\tilde{C}_{2}\rho^{1+M_{2}}\,\varpi_{M_{2}}\,\,\,&\text{for }\rho_{*}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
\hat{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu\quad&\text{for }\rho\geq\rho_{*},\end{cases}

(4.46)

provided (
4.36
), (
4.38
), (
4.40
), (
4.42
), and (
4.45
) hold.

To use the induction arguments, we make the induction assumption for
n=k
: For
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(k)}-V_{i}^{(k-1)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|\leq\begin{cases}\bar{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu^{k}\quad&\text{for }\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
\tilde{C}_{2}\rho^{1+M_{2}}\,\varpi_{M_{2}}\,\,\,&\text{for }\rho_{*}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
\hat{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu^{k}\quad&\text{for }\rho\geq\rho_{*}.\end{cases}

(4.47)

To estimate
|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(k+1)}-V_{i}^{(k)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)|
,
it suffices to consider the case:
\rho_{i}\leq\rho_{*}<\rho^{*}\leq\rho

for simplicity of presentation, since the other cases can be estimated
by similar arguments in (
4.1
)–(
4.45
).
In fact, for the case:
\rho_{i}\leq\rho_{*}<\rho^{*}\leq\rho
,
it follows from (
4.28
) and (
4.47
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(k+1)}-V_{i}^{(k)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho_{*}}+\int_{\rho_{*}}^{\rho^{*}}+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}\Big)\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(s)}{2k^{\prime}(s)}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{j}^{(k)}-V_{j}^{(k-1)})}{\partial u}(s,u^{(i)}(s))\Big|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad\,\,+\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{i})}{2k^{\prime}(\rho_{i})}\Big|\,\Big|\frac{\partial\rho_{i}}{\partial u}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\big|(V_{j}^{(k)}-V_{j}^{(k-1)})(\rho_{i},u_{i})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho_{*}}\bar{C}_{2}\nu^{k+1}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\rho_{*}}^{\rho^{*}}C_{0}\tilde{C}_{2}\varpi_{M_{2}}^{k}s^{+M_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}\hat{C}_{2}\nu^{k+1}\,\mathrm{d}s+C_{2}\nu\rho_{i}^{-\theta_{1}}\,C_{1}\varpi_{M_{2}}^{k}\rho_{i}^{1+\theta_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}_{2}(\rho-\rho^{*})\,\nu^{k+1}+\tilde{C}_{2}\big((\rho^{*})^{1+M_{2}}-(\rho_{*})^{1+M_{2}}\big)\varpi_{M_{2}}^{k+1}+\bar{C}_{2}(\rho_{*}-\rho_{i})\nu^{k+1}+C_{1}C_{2}\rho_{i}\nu^{k+1}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu^{k+1},

where we have chosen

\displaystyle M_{1}\geq M_{2}+\theta_{2},\qquad\bar{C}_{2}\geq C_{1}C_{2},

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{2}\geq\max\Big\{\tilde{C}_{1}C_{2}(1+M_{2})(\rho^{*})^{M_{1}-M_{2}-\theta_{2}}\Big(\frac{1+M_{2}}{1+M_{1}}\Big)^{k},\,\bar{C}_{2}\frac{\big(\nu\varpi_{M_{2}}^{-1}\big)^{k+1}}{(\rho_{*})^{M_{2}}}\Big\},

(4.48)

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2}\geq\max\Big\{\hat{C}_{1}C_{2},\,\tilde{C}_{2}(\rho^{*})^{M_{2}}\big(\nu^{-1}\varpi_{M_{2}}\big)^{k+1}\Big\}.

Thus, under assumption (
4.48
), we conclude that, for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(k+1)}-V_{i}^{(k)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|\leq\begin{cases}\bar{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu^{k+1}\quad&\text{for }\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
\tilde{C}_{2}\rho^{1+M_{2}}\,\varpi_{M_{2}}^{k+1}\,\,\,&\text{for }\rho_{*}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
\hat{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu^{k+1}\quad&\text{for }\rho\geq\rho_{*}.\end{cases}

Combining (
4.27
) with (
4.46
)–(
4.48
) and taking

\displaystyle\varpi_{M_{2}}=\nu,\qquad\bar{C}_{2}=\max\{C_{2},\,C_{1}C_{2}\},

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{2}=\max\big\{\bar{C}_{2}(\rho_{*})^{-M_{2}},\tilde{C}_{1}C_{2}(1+M_{2})(\rho^{*})^{M_{1}-M_{2}-\theta_{2}}\big\},\quad\hat{C}_{2}=\max\big\{\tilde{C}_{2}(\rho^{*})^{M_{2}},\hat{C}_{1}C_{2}\big\},

we have proved that, for any
n\geq 1
and
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(n)}-V_{i}^{(n-1)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|\leq\begin{cases}\bar{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu^{n}\quad&\text{for }\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
\tilde{C}_{2}\rho^{1+M_{2}}\,\nu^{n}\,\,\,&\text{for }\rho_{*}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
\hat{C}_{2}\rho\,\nu^{n}\quad\,&\text{for }\rho\geq\rho_{*}.\end{cases}

(4.49)

Noting that
\nu<1
and
\rho\leq\rho_{0}
for
(\rho,u)\in\overline{\Sigma}
,
we know that
\big\{\frac{\partial V_{i}^{(n)}}{\partial u}\big\}
is uniformly convergent
in
\overline{\Sigma}
. It is direct to check that the limit function is
\frac{\partial V_{i}}{\partial u}
.
Due to the continuity and uniform convergence of
\{\frac{\partial V_{i}^{(n)}}{\partial u}\}
,
it is clear that
\frac{\partial V_{i}}{\partial u}
is continuous in
\overline{\Sigma}
.

On the other hand, it follows from (
4.9
) that

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{\partial V_{1}^{(n)}}{\partial\rho}=k^{\prime}(\rho)\frac{\partial V_{1}^{(n)}}{\partial u}-\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{2k^{\prime}(\rho)}\big(V_{1}^{(n-1)}+V_{2}^{(n-1)}\big),\\
&\frac{\partial V_{2}^{(n)}}{\partial\rho}=-k^{\prime}(\rho)\frac{\partial V_{2}^{(n)}}{\partial u}-\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{2k^{\prime}(\rho)}\big(V_{1}^{(n-1)}+V_{2}^{(n-1)}\big),\end{aligned}\right.

which, with (
4.14
), (
4.28
), and (
4.49
), yields that, for
k\geq 0
and
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(n)}-V_{i}^{(n-1)})}{\partial\rho}(\rho,u)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq k^{\prime}(\rho)\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(n)}-V_{i}^{(n-1)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|+\Big|\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{2k^{\prime}(\rho)}\Big|\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\big|(V_{j}^{(n)}-V_{j}^{(n-1)})(\rho,u)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}C\rho^{\theta_{1}}\,\nu^{n}\qquad&\text{for }\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
C\rho^{M_{1}}\,\nu^{n}\,\,\,&\text{for }\rho_{*}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
C\rho^{\theta_{2}}\,\nu^{n}\qquad&\text{for }\rho\geq\rho_{*},\end{cases}

(4.50)

for some large constant
C>0
.
Thus,
\frac{\partial V_{i}^{(n)}}{\partial\rho}
converges uniformly to
\frac{\partial V_{i}}{\partial\rho}
in
\overline{\Sigma}
. It is clear that
\frac{\partial V_{i}}{\partial\rho}
is continuous.
Therefore,
(V_{1}(\rho,u),V_{2}(\rho,u))
is a
C^{1}
–solution of the Goursat problem (
4.4
)–(
4.5
),
which implies that
\hat{\eta}
is a
C^{2}
–solution of (
4.2
).

4.
From (
4.34
) and (
4.49
), we obtain that, for
i=1,2
,

\Big|\frac{\partial V_{i}}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|\leq\Big|\frac{\partial V_{i}^{(0)}}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|+\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\Big|\frac{\partial(V_{i}^{(n)}-V_{i}^{(n-1)})}{\partial u}(\rho,u)\Big|\leq C\rho

(4.51)

for
\rho\geq 0
and
|u|\leq k(\rho)
.
Similarly, using (
4.1
), we see that, for
i=1,2
,

\Big|\frac{\partial V_{i}}{\partial\rho}\Big|\leq C\rho^{\theta(\rho)}\qquad\text{for $\rho\geq 0$ and $|u|\leq k(\rho)$}.

Therefore, for
|u|\leq k(\rho)
,
it follows from (
4.3
) and (
4.51
) that

\displaystyle|\hat{\eta}_{uu}(\rho,u)|=|\partial_{u}V_{1}(\rho,u)-\partial_{u}V_{2}(\rho,u)|\leq C\rho,

\displaystyle|\hat{\eta}_{\rho u}(\rho,u)|=|k^{\prime}(\rho)(\partial_{u}V_{1}(\rho,u)+\partial_{u}V_{2}(\rho,u))|\leq C\rho^{\theta(\rho)}.

If
\hat{\eta}_{m}
is regarded as a function of
(\rho,u)
, we have

|\hat{\eta}_{m\rho}(\rho,u)|\leq C\rho^{\theta(\rho)-1},\quad|\hat{\eta}_{mu}(\rho,u)|\leq C\qquad\,\,\text{ for }|u|\leq k(\rho).

If
\hat{\eta}_{m}
is regarded as a function of
(\rho,m)
, we see that, for
|u|\leq k(\rho)
,

\displaystyle|\hat{\eta}_{m\rho}(\rho,m)|=|\hat{\eta}_{m\rho}(\rho,u)+u\hat{\eta}_{mu}(\rho,u)|\leq C\rho^{\theta(\rho)-1},

\displaystyle|\hat{\eta}_{mm}(\rho,m)|=|\rho^{-1}\hat{\eta}_{mu}(\rho,u)|\leq C\rho^{-1}.

5.
We now prove the uniqueness of
\hat{\eta}
, which is equivalent to
the uniqueness of solutions of (
4.4
)–(
4.5
)
in the class of
C^{1}
–solutions satisfying (
4.1
).
Suppose that there exist two
C^{1}
solutions
(V_{1},V_{2})
and
(\tilde{V}_{1},\tilde{V}_{2})

of (
4.4
)–(
4.5
) satisfying the uniform estimate (
4.1
).
Then it follows from (
4.9
) that, for
i=1,2
,

V_{i}(\rho,u)-\tilde{V}_{i}(\rho,u)=-\int_{\rho_{i}}^{\rho}\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(s)}{2k^{\prime}(s)}\,\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\big(V_{j}(s,u^{(i)}(s))-\tilde{V}_{j}(s,u^{(i)}(s))\big)\,\mathrm{d}s.

(4.52)

Applying the uniform estimates (
4.1
) and similar arguments as in (
4.28
)–(
4.52
) yields

\displaystyle\max_{(\rho,u)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}\atop|u|\leq k(\rho)}\big|V_{i}(\rho,u)-\tilde{V}_{i}(\rho,u)\big|\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &C\rho^{1+\theta_{1}}\,\varpi_{1}^{n}\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\text{for }\rho\leq\rho_{*},\\
&C\rho^{1+M_{1}}\,\varpi_{M_{1}}^{n}\quad\,\text{for }\rho_{*}\leq\rho\leq\rho^{*},\\
&C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\,\varpi_{2}^{n}\qquad\,\text{for }\rho\geq\rho^{*},\end{aligned}\right.

for any
n\geq 0
, where
C\gg 1
is independent of
n
.
Taking
n\to\infty
, we obtain that

V_{i}(\rho,u)\equiv\tilde{V}_{i}(\rho,u)
for
|u|\leq k(\rho)
which,
with (
4.3
) and
\hat{\eta}(0,u)\equiv 0
, yields the uniqueness of
\hat{\eta}
.

6.
We now estimate the entropy flux
\hat{q}
.
It follows from (
2.12
) that, for all entropy pairs,

q_{\rho}=u\eta_{\rho}+\rho k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}\eta_{u},\qquad q_{u}=\rho\eta_{\rho}+u\eta_{u}.

(4.53)

Then there exists an entropy flux
\hat{q}(\rho,u)\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R})

corresponding to the special entropy
\hat{\eta}
:

\hat{q}(\rho,u)=\frac{1}{2}\rho|u|^{3}\pm\rho u\big(e(\rho)+\rho e^{\prime}(\rho)\big)\qquad\,\text{for }\pm u\geq k(\rho).

It follows from (
4.30
) and (
4.53
) that

|\hat{q}_{\rho}(\rho,u)|=|u\hat{\eta}_{\rho}+\rho k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}\hat{\eta}_{u}|\leq C\rho^{\gamma(\rho)+\theta(\rho)-1}

for
|u|\leq k(\rho)
,
which implies

|\hat{q}(\rho,u)|=\Big|\int_{\bar{\rho}}^{\rho}\hat{q}_{\rho}(s,u)\,\mathrm{d}s+\hat{q}(\bar{\rho},u)\Big|\leq C\rho^{\gamma(\rho)+\theta(\rho)}\qquad\,\text{for }|u|\leq k(\rho),

(4.54)

where
(\bar{\rho},u)
is the point satisfying
k(\bar{\rho})=|u|
.

For
|u|\leq k(\rho)
, it follows from (
4.31
) and (
4.54
) that

|\hat{q}-u\hat{\eta}|\leq|\hat{q}|+|u||\hat{\eta}|\leq C\rho^{\gamma(\rho)+\theta(\rho)}
.
In region
\{(\rho,u)\,:\,|u|\geq k(\rho)\}
,
it is direct to check that all the estimates in Lemma
4.1
hold
by using (
4.1
).
Therefore, the proof of Lemma
4.1
is now complete.
\hfill\square

4.2
Estimates of the weak entropy pairs

In order to show the compactness of the weak entropy dissipation measures below,
we now derive some estimates of the weak entropy pairs.
To achieve this, from (
2.15
)–(
2.16
), it requires
to analyze the entropy kernel and entropy flux kernel, respectively.

The entropy kernel
\chi=\chi(\rho,u,s)
is a fundamental solution of the entropy equation (
2.14
):

\left\{\begin{aligned} \displaystyle&\chi_{\rho\rho}-\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho^{2}}\chi_{uu}=0,\\
\displaystyle&\chi|_{\rho=0}=0,\quad\chi_{\rho}|_{\rho=0}=\delta_{u=s}.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.55)

As pointed out in
Chen-LeFloch-2000
that equation (
4.55
)
is invariant under the Galilean transformation, which implies that

\chi(\rho,u,s)=\chi(\rho,u-s,0)=\chi(\rho,0,s-u)
.
For simplicity, we write it as
\chi(\rho,u,s)=\chi(\rho,u-s)
below
when no confusion arises.

The corresponding entropy flux kernel
\sigma(\rho,u,s)
satisfies the Cauchy problem
for
\sigma-u\chi
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} \displaystyle&(\sigma-u\chi)_{\rho\rho}-\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho^{2}}(\sigma-u\chi)_{uu}=\frac{P^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}\chi_{u},\\
\displaystyle&(\sigma-u\chi)|_{\rho=0}=0,\quad(\sigma-u\chi)_{\rho}|_{\rho=0}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.56)

We recall from
Chen-LeFloch-2000
that
\sigma-u\chi

is also Galilean invariant.
From (
1.4
)–(
1.6
),
P(\rho)

satisfies all the conditions in
Chen-LeFloch-2000
;
Chen-LeFloch-2003
.

For later use, we introduce the definition of fractional
derivatives (
cf.

Chen-1986
;
Chen-LeFloch-2000
;
Lions-Perthame-Souganidis-1996
).
For any real
\alpha>0
, the fractional derivative
\partial_{s}^{\alpha}f
of a function
f=f(s)
is

\partial_{s}^{\alpha}f(s)=\Gamma(-\alpha)f*[s]_{+}^{-\alpha-1},

where
\Gamma(x)
is the Gamma function and the convolution should be understood
in the sense of distributions.
The following formula:

\partial_{s}^{\alpha}(sg(s))=s\partial_{s}^{\alpha+1}g+(\alpha+1)\partial_{s}^{\alpha}g

holds for fractional derivatives.
We now present two useful lemmas
for the entropy kernel
\chi(\rho,u)
and the entropy flux kernel
\sigma(\rho,u)
when
\rho
is bounded.

Lemma 4.2

(
(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, Theorems 2.1–2.2)
)
.

The entropy kernel
\chi(\rho,u)
admits the expansion
:

\chi(\rho,u)=a_{1}(\rho)G_{\lambda_{1}}(\rho,u)+a_{2}(\rho)G_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\rho,u)+g_{1}(\rho,u)\qquad\text{for $\rho\in[0,\infty)$},

(4.57)

where
k(\rho)=\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\sqrt{P^{\prime}(y)}}{y}\,\mathrm{d}y
and

\displaystyle G_{\lambda_{1}}(\rho,u)=[k(\rho)^{2}-u^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda_{1}},\qquad\lambda_{1}=\frac{3-\gamma_{1}}{2(\gamma_{1}-1)}>0,

(4.58)

\displaystyle a_{1}(\rho)=M_{\lambda_{1}}k(\rho)^{-\lambda_{1}}k^{\prime}(\rho)^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad M_{\lambda_{1}}=\left(\frac{2\lambda_{1}}{\sqrt{2\lambda_{1}+1}}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-z^{2})^{\lambda_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}z\right)^{-1},

\displaystyle a_{2}(\rho)=-\frac{1}{4(\lambda_{1}+1)}k(\rho)^{-\lambda_{1}-1}k^{\prime}(\rho)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{\rho}k(s)^{\lambda_{1}}k^{\prime}(s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Moreover,
\operatorname{supp}\chi(\rho,u)\subset\{(\rho,u)\,:\,|u|\leq k(\rho)\}
,
and
\chi(\rho,u)>0

in
\{(\rho,u)\,:\,|u|<k(\rho)\}
. The remainder term
g_{1}(\rho,\cdot)
and its fractional
derivative
\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{1}(\rho,\cdot)
are Hölder continuous.
Furthermore, for any fixed
\rho_{\max}>0
, there exists

C(\rho_{\max})>0

depending only on
\rho_{\rm max}
such that

|g_{1}(\rho,u-s)|\leq C(\rho_{\max})[k(\rho)^{2}-(u-s)^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda_{1}+\alpha_{0}+1},

(4.59)

for any
0\leq\rho\leq\rho_{\max}
and some
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
.
In addition, for any
0\leq\rho\leq\rho_{\max}
,

|a_{1}(\rho)|+\rho^{1-2\theta_{1}}|a_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2-2\theta_{1}}|a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|+|a_{2}(\rho)|+\rho|a_{2}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2}|a_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|\leq C(\rho_{\max}).

(4.60)

Proof.

Since (
4.57
)–(
4.59
) have been derived in
(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, Theorem 2.2)
,
it suffices to prove (
4.60
).
From (
3.6
), we find that

|a_{1}(\rho)|\leq C(\rho_{\max})
for
0\leq\rho\leq\rho_{\max}
.
For
|a_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)|
, a direct calculation shows that

a_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)=-\lambda_{1}M_{\lambda_{1}}k(\rho)^{-\lambda_{1}-1}k^{\prime}(\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{1}{2}M_{\lambda_{1}}k(\rho)^{-\lambda_{1}}k^{\prime}(\rho)^{-\frac{3}{2}}k^{\prime\prime}(\rho).

It follows from (
1.5
) that
k(\rho)=C_{1}\rho^{\theta_{1}}\big(1+O(\rho^{2\theta_{1}})\big)

as
\rho\in[0,\rho_{\max}]

for some constant
C_{1}>0
that may depend on
\kappa_{1}
and
\gamma_{1}
.
Then, by direct calculation, we observe that the term involving
\rho^{-1}
in
a_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)
vanishes
so that
|a_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)|\leq C(\rho_{\max})\rho^{2\theta_{1}-1}
.
Similarly, we obtain that
|a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|\leq C(\rho_{\max})\rho^{2\theta_{1}-2}
.
Finally, using
\eqref{6.5-1}_{3}
, we can obtain the estimates
of
a_{2}(\rho)
in
(
4.60
)
by a direct calculation.
This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

Lemma 4.3

(
(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, Theorem 2.3)
)
.

The entropy flux kernel
\sigma(\rho,u)
admits the expansion

(\sigma-u\chi)(\rho,u)=-u\big(b_{1}(\rho)G_{\lambda_{1}}(\rho,u)+b_{2}(\rho)G_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\rho,u)\big)+g_{2}(\rho,u)\qquad\text{for $\rho\in[0,\infty)$},

where

\displaystyle b_{1}(\rho)=M_{\lambda_{1}}\rho k(\rho)^{-\lambda_{1}-1}k^{\prime}(\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}>0,

(4.61)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} b_{2}(\rho)&=-\frac{1}{4(\lambda_{1}+1)}\rho k^{\prime}(\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}k(\rho)^{-(\lambda_{1}+2)}\int_{0}^{\rho}k(s)^{\lambda_{1}}k^{\prime}(s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\quad-\frac{1}{4(\lambda_{1}+1)}k(\rho)^{-(\lambda_{1}+2)}k^{\prime}(\rho)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{\rho}k(s)^{\lambda_{1}+1}k^{\prime}(s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}b_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{4(\lambda_{1}+1)}k(\rho)^{-(\lambda_{1}+2)}k^{\prime}(\rho)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{\rho}sk(s)^{\lambda_{1}}k^{\prime}(s)^{\frac{1}{2}}a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{aligned}

The remainder term
g_{2}(\rho,\cdot)
and its fractional derivative
\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{2}(\rho,\cdot)

are Hölder continuous.
Moreover, for any fixed
\rho_{\max}>0
, there exists
C(\rho_{\max})>0

depending only on
\rho_{max}
such that

|g_{2}(\rho,u-s)|\leq C(\rho_{\max})[k(\rho)^{2}-(u-s)^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda_{1}+\alpha_{0}+1},

for any
0\leq\rho\leq\rho_{\max}
and some
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
.
Furthermore, similar to the proof of (
4.60
), for any
0\leq\rho\leq\rho_{\max}
,

|b_{1}(\rho)|+\rho^{1-2\theta_{1}}|b_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2-2\theta_{1}}|b_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|+|b_{2}(\rho)|+\rho|b_{2}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2}|b_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|\leq C(\rho_{\max}).

(4.62)

Remark 4.1
.

In
(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, Theorem 2.2)
, it is proved
that
a_{2}(\rho)
and
b_{2}(\rho)
satisfy
|a_{2}(\rho)|+|b_{2}(\rho)|\leq C\rho k(\rho)^{-2}

for the pressure law given in
(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, (2.1))
. In this paper, we have improved them
to be
(
4.60
)
and
(
4.62
)
under conditions (
1.4
)–(
1.6
).

For later use, we recall a useful representation formula for
\chi(\rho,u)
.

Lemma 4.4

(First representation formula,
(
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
, Lemma 3.4)
)
.

Given any
(\rho,u)
with
|u|\leq k(\rho)
and
0\leq\rho_{0}<\rho
,

\displaystyle\chi(\rho,u)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho_{0})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho}k^{\prime}(s)\,\tilde{d}(s)\big(\chi(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))+\chi(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho_{0})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{-(k(\rho)-k(\rho_{0}))}^{k(\rho)-k(\rho_{0})}\chi(\rho_{0},u-s)\,\mathrm{d}s,

where

\,\tilde{d}(\rho):=2+(\rho-\rho_{0})\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{k^{\prime}(\rho)}.

Remark 4.2
.

In the statement of
(
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
, Lemma 3.4)
,
\rho_{0}
is positive.
However, the proof of
(
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
, Lemma 3.4)
is also valid
for
\rho_{0}=0
without modification
;
see also

(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, (3.38))
.

Given any
\psi\in C_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, a regular weak entropy pair
(\eta^{\psi},\,q^{\psi})
can be given by

\displaystyle\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(s)\,\chi(\rho,u,s)\,\mathrm{d}s,\qquad q^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(s)\,\sigma(\rho,u,s)\,\mathrm{d}s.

(4.63)

It follows from (
4.55
) that

\left\{\begin{aligned} \displaystyle&\eta_{\rho\rho}^{\psi}-k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}\eta_{uu}^{\psi}=0,\\
\displaystyle&\eta^{\psi}|_{\rho=0}=0,\quad\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}|_{\rho=0}=\psi(u).\end{aligned}\right.

(4.64)

Using Lemmas
4.2
–
4.4
, we
obtain the following lemma
for the weak entropy pair
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
.

Lemma 4.5
.

For any weak entropy
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
defined in (
4.63
),
there exists a constant
C_{\psi}>0
depending only on
\rho^{*}
and
\psi

such that, for all
\rho\in[0,2\rho^{*}]
,

|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho.

If
\eta^{\psi}
is regarded as a function of
(\rho,m)
, then

\displaystyle|\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|+|\rho\eta_{mm}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi},\qquad|\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\big(1+\rho^{\theta_{1}}\big).

Moreover, if
\eta_{m}^{\psi}
is regarded as a function of
(\rho,u)
, then

|\eta_{mu}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|\rho^{1-\theta_{1}}\eta_{m\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}.

Proof.
All the estimates can be found in
(
Schrecker-Schulz-2019
, Lemma 3.8)

or
(
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
, Lemma 4.13)
except the estimate of
\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,m)
.
In fact, applying Lemma
4.4
to (
4.64
) and using

\displaystyle d(\rho):=2+\frac{\rho k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{k^{\prime}(\rho)}
, we have

\displaystyle\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2\rho k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{0}^{\rho}k^{\prime}(s)\,d(s)\,\eta^{\psi}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\,+\frac{1}{2\rho k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{0}^{\rho}k^{\prime}(s)\,d(s)\,\eta^{\psi}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s:=I_{1}+I_{2}.

(4.65)

We regard
\eta^{\psi}
as a function of
(\rho,m)
. Then we have

\partial_{\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)=\partial_{\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)-\frac{u}{\rho}\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u).

(4.66)

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\operatorname{supp}\psi\subset[-L,L]
for some
L>0
.
Then a direct calculation shows that
\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)=0
if
|u|\geq k(\rho)+L
.
Noticing
\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\rho\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)
, we have

\Big|\frac{u}{\rho}\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)\Big|\leq|u|\,|\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}(1+\rho^{\theta_{1}})\qquad\mbox{for }0\leq\rho\leq 2\rho^{*}.

(4.67)

Thus, it suffices to calculate
\partial_{\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)
. It follows from (
4.65
) that

\partial_{\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\partial_{\rho}I_{1}+\partial_{\rho}I_{2}
. A direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}I_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big(-\rho^{-2}(k^{\prime}(\rho))^{-1}-\rho^{-1}(k^{\prime}(\rho))^{-2}k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)\big)\int_{0}^{\rho}k^{\prime}(s)\,d(s)\,\eta^{\psi}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}k^{\prime}(s)\,d(s)\,\eta_{u}^{\psi}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2\rho}d(\rho)\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u).

(4.68)

Using (
3.6
) and Lemma
3.2
, we obtain that

|\partial_{\rho}I_{1}|\leq C_{\psi}(1+\rho^{\theta_{1}})\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $0\leq\rho\leq 2\rho^{*}$}.

Similarly, we obtain that

|\partial_{\rho}I_{2}|\leq C_{\psi}(1+\rho^{\theta_{1}})
.
Thus, we conclude that

|\partial_{\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq|\partial_{\rho}I_{1}|+|\partial_{\rho}I_{2}|\leq C_{\psi}(1+\rho^{\theta_{1}}),

which, with (
4.66
)–(
4.67
), implies that

|\partial_{\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}(1+\rho^{\theta_{1}}).

\hfill\square

We notice that all the above estimates for the weak entropy pairs
in Lemmas
4.2
–
4.5
hold when the density is bounded.
To establish the
L^{p}
-compensated compactness framework, we need the entropy pair estimates
when the density is large, namely
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
.
From now on in this subsection,
we use the representation formula of Lemma
4.4
to estimate
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})

in the large density region
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

There exists a positive constant
C>0
depending only on
\rho^{*}
such that

\|\chi(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\leq C\rho\qquad\text{for $\rho\geq\rho^{*}$}.

Proof.

For
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,
\chi(\rho,u)
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\displaystyle\chi_{\rho\rho}-k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}\chi_{uu}=0,\\
&\displaystyle\chi(\rho,u)|_{\rho=\rho^{*}}=\chi(\rho^{*},u),\quad\chi_{\rho}(\rho,u)|_{\rho=\rho^{*}}=\chi_{\rho}(\rho^{*},u).\end{aligned}\right.

where
\chi(\rho^{*},u)
and
\chi_{\rho}(\rho^{*},u)
are given in Lemma
4.2
.
Then, applying Lemma
4.4
,
we obtain that, for
\rho>\rho^{*}
,

\displaystyle\|k^{\prime}(\rho)\chi(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho-\rho^{*}}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\|k^{\prime}(s)\chi(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho^{*})}\int_{-(k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*}))}^{k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*})}\|\chi(\rho^{*},\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho-\rho^{*}}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\|k^{\prime}(s)\chi(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\rho^{\theta_{2}-1},

where

d_{*}(\rho):=2+(\rho-\rho^{*})\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{k^{\prime}(\rho)}
.
By a similar proof to that for Lemma
10.3
,
we have

\|k^{\prime}(\rho)\chi(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\leq C\rho^{\theta_{2}}\qquad\text{for $\rho\geq 2\rho^{*}$},

which, with (
3.7
), yields that

\|\chi(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\leq C\rho

for
\rho\geq 2\rho^{*}
.
For
\rho^{*}\leq\rho\leq 2\rho^{*}
, it follows from Lemma
4.2
that

\|\chi(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\leq C\leq C\rho
.

\hfill\square

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
and
\psi\in C_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Then, in the
(\rho,u)
–coordinates,

|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|\eta_{uu}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho,\quad|\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+\rho^{1-\theta_{2}}|\eta_{\rho\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}}.

In the
(\rho,m)
–coordinates,

\,|\eta^{\psi}_{\rho}(\rho,m)|+\rho^{\theta_{2}}|\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|+\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}|\eta_{mm}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}}
.

If
\eta^{\psi}_{m}(\rho,m)
is regarded as a function of
(\rho,u)
, then

\,|\eta_{mu}^{\psi}|+\rho^{1-\theta_{2}}|\eta_{m\rho}^{\psi}|\leq C_{\psi}
.

All the above constants
C_{\psi}>0
depend only on
\|\psi\|_{C^{2}}
and
\operatorname{supp}\psi
.

Proof.
We divide the proof into five steps.

1. Using (
4.63
) and Lemma
4.6
,
we obtain that, for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,

|{\eta}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|{\eta}_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|{\eta}_{uu}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq\|{\chi}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|(\psi,\psi^{\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{\psi}\rho.

(4.69)

2. For the estimate of
\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)
, the proof is similar to Lemma
4.5
. Indeed,

\eta^{\psi}
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} \displaystyle&\eta_{\rho\rho}^{\psi}-k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}\eta_{uu}^{\psi}=0,\\
\displaystyle&\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|_{\rho=\rho^{*}}=\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{*},u),\quad\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|_{\rho=\rho^{*}}=\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho^{*},u).\end{aligned}\right.

(4.70)

It follows from (
4.70
) and Lemma
4.4
that

\displaystyle\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)k^{\prime}(s)\eta^{\psi}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)k^{\prime}(s)\eta^{\psi}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}\left(\rho\right)}\int_{-(k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*}))}^{k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*})}\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{*},u-s)\,\mathrm{d}s,

(4.71)

where
d_{*}(\rho)=2+(\rho-\rho^{*})\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{k^{\prime}(\rho)}
and

0<d_{*}(\rho)\leq 3

for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
from (
3.10
).
Then, following the similar arguments as in the proof of Lemma
4.5
,
we can obtain that
|\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}}
for
\rho\geq 2\rho^{*}
.
Moreover, from Lemma
4.5
,

|\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}}
for
\rho\in[\rho^{*},2\rho^{*}]
so that

|\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}}\qquad\text{for }\rho\geq\rho^{*}.

(4.72)

3.
For
\eta_{\rho\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)
, it follows from (
4.69
)–(
4.70
) that

|\eta_{\rho\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq|k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}|\,|\eta_{uu}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{2\theta_{2}-1}=C_{\psi}\rho^{\gamma_{2}-2}\qquad\text{ for }\rho\geq\rho^{*}.

4.
In the
(\rho,m)
–coordinates, it is clear that

\displaystyle\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)=\rho^{-1}\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u),\quad\eta_{mm}^{\psi}(\rho,m)=\rho^{-2}\eta_{uu}^{\psi}(\rho,u),\quad\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,m)=\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)-\frac{m}{\rho^{2}}\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u).

On the other hand, if
\eta_{m}^{\psi}
is regarded as a function
(\rho,u)
, it is direct to obtain

\eta_{mu}^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\partial_{u}\big(\rho^{-1}\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)\big)=\rho^{-1}\eta_{uu}^{\psi}(\rho,u).

Thus, using (
4.69
) and (
4.72
),

\displaystyle|\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|+|\eta_{mu}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+\rho|\eta_{mm}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{-1},

\displaystyle|\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq|\eta_{\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+\frac{|m|}{\rho^{2}}\big|\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)\big|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}}+C_{\psi}(L+k(\rho))\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}},

where we have used that
\operatorname{supp}\psi\subset[-L,L]

and
\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\rho\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)
.

5.
For the estimates of
\eta_{m\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\partial_{\rho}\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,u)
, it follows from (
4.71
) that

\displaystyle\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)=\frac{1}{\rho}\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\,k^{\prime}(s)\,\eta_{u}^{\psi}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\,k^{\prime}(s)\,\eta_{u}^{\psi}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{-(k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*}))}^{k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*})}\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho^{*},u-s)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle:=J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3},

(4.73)

and
\partial_{\rho}\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\partial_{\rho}J_{1}+\partial_{\rho}J_{2}+\partial_{\rho}J_{3}
.

A direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}J_{1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}\Big(\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\Big)\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\,k^{\prime}(s)\,\eta_{u}^{\psi}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\,k^{\prime}(s)\,\eta_{uu}^{\psi}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})}d_{*}(\rho)\,\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle:=J_{1,1}+J_{1,2}+J_{1,3},

(4.74)

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}J_{2}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}\Big(\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\Big)\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\,k^{\prime}(s)\,\eta_{u}^{\psi}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\,k^{\prime}(s)\,\eta_{uu}^{\psi}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})}d_{*}(\rho)\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle:=J_{2,1}+J_{2,2}+J_{2,3}.

(4.75)

Clearly, we have

\Big|\partial_{\rho}\Big(\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\Big)\Big|=\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{\rho-\rho^{*}}{2\rho^{2}\,(\rho-\rho^{*})^{2}\,k^{\prime}(\rho)}+\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{\rho\,(\rho-\rho^{*})\,k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}}\Big|\leq\frac{C}{(\rho-\rho^{*})^{2}\rho^{\theta_{2}}},

which, with (
4.69
) and
0<d_{*}(\rho)\leq 3
for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,
yields

\displaystyle|J_{1,1}+J_{2,1}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\partial_{\rho}\Big(\frac{1}{2\rho\,(\rho-\rho^{*})\,k^{\prime}(\rho)}\Big)\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}k^{\prime}(s)\,d_{*}(s)\,\eta_{u}^{\psi}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\partial_{\rho}\Big(\frac{1}{2\rho\,(\rho-\rho^{*})\,k^{\prime}(\rho)}\Big)\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}k^{\prime}(s)\,d_{*}(s)\,\eta_{u}^{\psi}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\psi}}{(\rho-\rho^{*})^{2}\,\rho^{\theta_{2}}}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}s^{\theta_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C_{\psi}}{\rho-\rho^{*}}.

(4.76)

It follows from (
4.69
) and
0<d_{*}(\rho)\leq 3
for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}

that

\displaystyle|J_{1,2}|+|J_{2,2}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\,k^{\prime}(s)\,\eta_{uu}^{\psi}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

(4.77)

\displaystyle+\Big|\frac{1}{2\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\,k^{\prime}(s)\,\eta_{uu}^{\psi}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\psi}}{\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}s^{\theta_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C_{\psi}}{\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})}\rho^{\theta_{2}}(\rho-\rho^{*})\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}-1}.

For
J_{1,3}+J_{2,3}
, it is direct to see that

|J_{1,3}+J_{2,3}|\leq\Big|\frac{1}{\rho(\rho-\rho^{*})}\,d_{*}(\rho)\,\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho,u)\Big|\leq\frac{C_{\psi}}{\rho-\rho^{*}}.

(4.78)

For
\partial_{\rho}J_{3}
, we notice that

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}J_{3}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\rho}\Big(\frac{1}{2\rho\,(\rho-\rho^{*})\,k^{\prime}(\rho)}\Big)\int_{-(k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*}))}^{k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*})}\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho^{*},u-s)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\rho\,(\rho-\rho^{*})}\,\big(\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho^{*},u-k(\rho)+k(\rho^{*}))+\eta_{u}^{\psi}(\rho^{*},u+k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*}))\big),

which, with
0<\theta_{2}\leq 1
, yields

\displaystyle|\partial_{\rho}J_{3}|\leq\frac{C_{\psi}\rho^{*}}{(\rho-\rho^{*})^{2}}\,|k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*})|+\frac{C_{\psi}\rho^{*}}{\rho\,(\rho-\rho^{*})}\leq\frac{C_{\psi}}{\rho-\rho^{*}}(1+\rho^{\theta_{2}-1})\leq\frac{C_{\psi}}{\rho-\rho^{*}}.

(4.79)

Combining (
4.74
)–(
4.79
) with (
4.73
) yields
that

|\eta_{m\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}-1}
for
\rho\geq 2\rho^{*}
.
For
\rho^{*}\leq\rho\leq 2\rho^{*}
, it follows from Lemma
4.5
that

|\eta_{m\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{1}-1}\leq C_{\psi}

for
\rho^{*}\leq\rho\leq 2\rho^{*}
.
Thus, we conclude that

|\eta_{m\rho}^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}-1}

for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
.

\hfill\square

We now estimate
q^{\psi}
for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
. It follows from (
4.56
) that
h:=\sigma-u\chi
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} \displaystyle&h_{\rho\rho}-k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}h_{uu}=\frac{P^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}\chi_{u},\\
\displaystyle&h(\rho^{*},u)=(\sigma-u\chi)(\rho^{*},u),\quad h_{\rho}(\rho^{*},u)=(\sigma-u\chi)_{\rho}(\rho^{*},u),\end{aligned}\right.

where
(\sigma-u\chi)(\rho^{*},u)
and
(\sigma-u\chi)_{\rho}(\rho^{*},u)
are given by Lemma
4.3
.
Similar to Lemma
4.4
, we have the following representation formula for
h
.

Lemma 4.8

(Second representation formula
(
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
, Lemmas 3.4 and 3.9)
)
.

For any
(\rho,u)
with
|u|\leq k(\rho)
and
\rho>\rho^{*}
,

\displaystyle h(\rho,u)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}k^{\prime}(s)d_{*}(s)\big(h(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))+h(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}(s-\rho^{*})\frac{P^{\prime\prime}(s)}{s}\chi(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}(s-\rho^{*})\frac{P^{\prime\prime}(s)}{s}\chi(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2(\rho-\rho^{*})k^{\prime}(\rho)}\int_{-(k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*}))}^{k(\rho)-k(\rho^{*})}h(\rho^{*},u-s)\,\mathrm{d}s,

(4.80)

where
d_{*}(\rho)=2+(\rho-\rho^{*})\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{k^{\prime}(\rho)}
.

Lemma 4.9
.

There exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
\rho^{*}
such that

\|(\sigma-u\chi)(\rho,u)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\qquad\,\,\text{for }\rho\geq\rho^{*}.

Proof.

It follows from (
3.3
), (
4.8
), and Lemma
4.6
that

\displaystyle\|k^{\prime}(\rho)h(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho-\rho^{*}}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\|k^{\prime}(s)h(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C}{\rho-\rho^{*}}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}s^{\gamma_{2}-1}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\rho^{\theta_{2}-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho-\rho^{*}}\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}d_{*}(s)\|k^{\prime}(s)h(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\rho^{2\theta_{2}},

which, with (
3.7
) and a similar proof to that for Lemma
10.3
,
yields

\|k^{\prime}(\rho)h(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\leq C\rho^{2\theta_{2}}\,\Longrightarrow\,\|h(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\qquad\text{for }\rho\geq 2\rho^{*}.

For
\rho^{*}\leq\rho\leq 2\rho^{*}
, it follow from Lemma
4.3
that

\|h(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}}\leq C\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}
.

\hfill\square

Lemma 4.10
.

For
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
and
\psi\in C_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
,

|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}.

(4.81)

Proof.
Recall that

\displaystyle q^{\psi}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\sigma(\rho,u,s)-u\chi(\rho,u-s)\big)\psi(s)\,\mathrm{d}s+u\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi(\rho,u-s)\psi(s)\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.82)

\displaystyle:=h^{\psi}(\rho,u)+u\,\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u).

It follows from Lemma
4.9

that

|h^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C\|(\sigma-u\chi)(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|\psi\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}.

(4.83)

Since there exists

L>0
such that
\operatorname{supp}\psi\subset[-L,L]
,
then it follows from Lemma
4.7
that

|u\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq(k(\rho)+L)|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}

for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,
which, with (
4.82
)–(
4.83
), yields (
4.81
).

\hfill\square

4.3
Singularities of the entropy kernel and the entropy flux kernel

As indicated in
Chen-LeFloch-2000
;
Schrecker-Schulz-2019
;
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
,
understanding the singularities of the entropy kernel and the entropy flux kernel is essential for the reduction
of the Young measure. Thus, it requires some detailed estimates of the singularities of the entropy kernel
and the entropy flux kernel.
The arguments in this subsection are similar to
(
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
, §6)
,
the main difference is that a more subtle Grönwall inequality (see Lemma
10.3
) is needed
to obtain the desired estimates of the singularities.

Lemma 4.11
.

For
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
, the coefficient functions
a_{1}(\rho)
and
a_{2}(\rho)

and the remainder term
g_{1}(\rho,u)
in
Lemma
4.2
satisfy

\displaystyle|a_{1}(\rho)|+\rho^{\theta_{2}}|a_{2}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}},\qquad\,\,\|g_{1}(\rho,u)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\begin{cases}C\rho\quad&\text{if $\theta_{2}<\theta_{1}$},\\
C\rho\ln\rho\,\,\,&\text{if $\theta_{2}=\theta_{1}$},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\|\partial_{u}g_{1}(\rho,u)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{1}(\rho,u)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{1}(\rho,u)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho,

where
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
is the Hölder exponent.

Proof.
We divide the proof into five steps.

1.
It follows from (
4.60
) that, for
\rho\in[0,\rho_{*}]
,

|a_{1}(\rho)|+\rho^{1-2\theta_{1}}|a_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2-2\theta_{1}}|a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|+|a_{2}(\rho)|+\rho|a_{2}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2}|a_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|\leq C.

(4.84)

For
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
, it follows from (
3.7
), (
4.58
), and a direct calculation that

\displaystyle|a_{1}(\rho)|+\rho|a_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2}|a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}},

(4.85)

\displaystyle|a_{2}(\rho)|=\Big|\frac{1}{4\lambda_{1}+1}k(\rho)^{-\lambda_{1}-1}k^{\prime}(\rho)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{\rho}k(s)^{\lambda_{1}}k^{\prime}(s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}-\theta_{2}}\Big(\rho_{*}^{\theta_{1}}+1+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}s^{-1-\theta_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\theta_{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}}.

(4.86)

Moreover, calculating the derivatives explicitly, we conclude

|a_{2}(\rho)|+\rho|a_{2}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2}|a_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\theta_{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}}\qquad\,\,\text{for }\rho\geq\rho^{*}.

2. For the remainder term
g_{1}(\rho,u)
, it follows from
(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, Proof of Theorem 2.1)
that

\left\{\begin{aligned} \displaystyle&\partial_{\rho\rho}g_{1}(\rho,u)-k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}\partial_{uu}g_{1}(\rho,u)=A(\rho)k(\rho)^{-1}f_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\frac{u}{k(\rho)}),\\
\displaystyle&g_{1}(0,\cdot)=0,\quad\partial_{\rho}g_{1}(0,\cdot)=0,\end{aligned}\right.

where
f_{\lambda_{1}}(y)=[1-y^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda_{1}}
and
A(\rho)=-a_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)k(\rho)^{2\lambda_{1}+3}
.
By (
4.84
),

A(\rho)\sim O(\rho^{-1+2\theta_{1}})
as
\rho\to 0
and
|A(\rho)|\leq\rho^{-\frac{3}{2}+\theta_{2}(1+\frac{1}{2\theta_{1}})}
for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
.
Similar to Lemma
4.4
, we have the following representation formula for
g_{1}(\rho,u)
:

\displaystyle k^{\prime}(\rho)g_{1}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)\,k^{\prime}(s)\,\big(g_{1}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))+g_{1}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}s\,A(s)\,k(s)^{-1}\,\Big(\int_{u-k(\rho)+k(s)}^{u+k(\rho)-k(s)}f_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\frac{y}{k(s)})\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s,

(4.87)

where
d(\rho)=2+\rho\frac{k^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{k^{\prime}(\rho)}
satisfying (
3.11
).
Since
\rho A(\rho)k(\rho)^{-1}\sim O(\rho^{\theta_{1}})
as
\rho\to 0
,
the second integral is well-defined.
Then it follows directly from (
3.6
)–(
3.7
) and (
4.87
) that

\displaystyle\|k^{\prime}(\rho)g_{1}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)\|k^{\prime}(s)g_{1}(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{2k(\rho)}{\rho}\Big(\int_{0}^{\rho_{*}}+\int_{\rho_{*}}^{\rho^{*}}+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}\Big)|sA(s)k(s)^{-1}|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)\|k^{\prime}(s)g_{1}(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\rho^{\theta_{2}-1}\big(\rho_{*}^{1+\theta_{1}}+1+\rho^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)\|k^{\prime}(s)g_{1}(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}+\theta_{2}}\qquad\,\,\text{for }\rho\geq\rho^{*}.

(4.88)

Since
g_{1}(\rho,u)
is Hölder continuous and
\operatorname{supp}g_{1}(\rho,\cdot)\subset[-k(\rho),k(\rho)]
,
it follows from (
3.6
)–(
3.7
) that

\|k^{\prime}(\rho)g_{1}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
is locally integrable with respect to
\rho\in[0,\infty)
.
Applying Lemma
10.3
to (
4.88
), we obtain that, for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,

\|k^{\prime}(\rho)g_{1}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &C\rho^{\theta_{2}}\qquad\quad\;\;\text{if $\theta_{2}<\theta_{1}$},\\
&C\rho^{\theta_{2}}\ln\rho\qquad\text{if $\theta_{2}=\theta_{1}$},\end{aligned}\right.

which, with (
3.7
), yields that, for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,

\|g_{1}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &C\rho\qquad\quad\;\;\text{if }\theta_{2}<\theta_{1},\\
&C\rho\ln\rho\qquad\text{if }\theta_{2}=\theta_{1}.\end{aligned}\right.

3. Applying
\partial_{u}
to (
4.87
), we have

\displaystyle k^{\prime}(\rho)\partial_{u}g_{1}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)k^{\prime}(s)\Big(\partial_{u}g_{1}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))+\partial_{u}g_{1}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sA(s)k(s)^{-1}f_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\frac{u+k(\rho)-k(s)}{k(s)})\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sA(s)k(s)^{-1}f_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\frac{u-k(\rho)+k(s)}{k(s)})\,\mathrm{d}s.

(4.89)

Since
|f_{\lambda_{1}+1}(s)|\leq 1
, by similar arguments as in Step 2, we can obtain

\|\partial_{u}g_{1}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho\qquad\,\,\text{for }\rho\geq\rho^{*}.

4. Applying the fractional derivative
\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}}
to (
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle k^{\prime}(\rho)\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{1}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)k^{\prime}(s)\Big((\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{1})(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))+(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{1})(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sA(s)k(s)^{-1-\lambda_{1}}(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}}f_{\lambda_{1}+1})(\frac{u+k(\rho)-k(s)}{k(s)})\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sA(s)k(s)^{-1-\lambda_{1}}(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}}f_{\lambda_{1}+1})(\frac{u-k(\rho)+k(s)}{k(s)})\,\mathrm{d}s,

where we have taken into account the homogeneity of the factional derivative in the last term.
Using the Fourier transform relation as in
(
Lions-Perthame-Souganidis-1996
, (I.26)–(I.27))
, we can obtain

\big|\mathscr{F}\big((\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}}f_{\lambda_{1}+1})(u)\big)(\xi)\big|=C_{\lambda_{1}+1}|\xi|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,|J_{\lambda_{1}+\frac{3}{2}}(|\xi|)|\leq\frac{\tilde{C}_{\lambda_{1}+1}}{1+\xi^{2}}

for some positive constants
C_{\lambda_{1}+1}
and
\tilde{C}_{\lambda_{1}+1}
depending only on
\lambda_{1}+1
,
where we have used the asymptotic relations for the first kind of Bessel functions
J_{\lambda_{1}+\frac{3}{2}}(|\xi|)

to obtain the final inequality.
Since
(1+|\xi|^{2})^{-1}
is integrable, applying the Fourier inversion theorem,
we see that
(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}}f_{\lambda_{1}+1})(u)
is uniformly bounded.
Hence, by similar arguments as in Step 2, we have

\|\big(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{1}\big)(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho\qquad\,\,\text{for $\rho\geq\rho^{*}$}.

5. By Lemma
4.2
, we assume that
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
is the Hölder exponent of
(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1})g_{1}(\rho,u)
.
Then, applying the fractional derivative
\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}}
to (
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle k^{\prime}(\rho)\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{1}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)k^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{1})(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)k^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{1})(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sA(s)k(s)^{-1-\lambda_{1}-\alpha_{0}}(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+\alpha_{0}}f_{\lambda_{1}+1})(\frac{u+k(\rho)-k(s)}{k(s)})\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sA(s)k(s)^{-1-\lambda_{1}-\alpha_{0}}(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+\alpha_{0}}f_{\lambda_{1}+1})(\frac{u-k(\rho)+k(s)}{k(s)})\,\mathrm{d}s.

Noting

\big|\mathscr{F}\big((\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+\alpha_{0}}f_{\lambda_{1}+1})(u)\big)(\xi)\big|=C_{\lambda_{1}+1}|\xi|^{-\frac{3}{2}+\alpha_{0}}\big|J_{\lambda_{1}+\frac{3}{2}}(|\xi|)\big|\leq\frac{\tilde{C}_{\lambda_{1}+1}}{1+\xi^{2-\alpha_{0}}},

and using the Fourier inversion theorem, we find that

(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+\alpha_{0}}f_{\lambda_{1}+1})(u)
is uniformly bounded.
By similar arguments as in Step 2, we obtain that

\|\big(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{1}\big)(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho

for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
.
This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

From Lemmas
4.2
and
4.11
, we conclude

Corollary 4.12
.

\chi(\rho,\cdot)
is Hölder continuous and

\|\chi(\rho,\cdot)\|_{C_{u}^{\tilde{\alpha}}}\leq C(1+\rho|\ln\rho|)\qquad\,\,\text{for $\,\tilde{\alpha}\in(0,\min\{\lambda_{1},1\}]\text{ and }\rho\geq 0$}.

Lemma 4.13
.

For
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
, the coefficient functions
b_{1}(\rho)
and
b_{2}(\rho)

and the remainder term
g_{2}(\rho,u)
in
Lemma
4.3
satisfy

\displaystyle|b_{1}(\rho)|+\rho^{\theta_{2}}|b_{2}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}},\qquad\,\,\|g_{2}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\begin{cases}C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\quad&\text{if $\theta_{2}<\theta_{1}$},\\
C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\ln\rho\,\,\,&\text{if $\theta_{2}=\theta_{1}$},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\|\partial_{u}g_{2}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\big(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{2}\big)(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\big(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{2}\big)(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}},

where
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
is the Hölder exponent.

Proof.
We divide the proof into five steps.

1.
It follows from (
4.62
) that, for
\rho\in[0,\rho_{*}]
,

|b_{1}(\rho)|+\rho^{1-2\theta_{1}}|b_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2-2\theta_{1}}|b_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|+|b_{2}(\rho)|+\rho|b_{2}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2}|b_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|\leq C.

(4.90)

From (
4.61
) and (
3.7
), we have

|b_{1}(\rho)|+|\rho b_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)|+|\rho^{2}b_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}}\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $\rho\geq\rho^{*}$}.

(4.91)

Using (
4.84
)–(
4.85
) and (
4.90
)–(
4.91
), we obtain that, for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{\rho}k(s)^{\lambda_{1}}k^{\prime}(s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq C\int_{0}^{\rho^{*}}s^{-1+\theta_{1}}\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{\rho_{*}}^{\rho}s^{-1-\theta_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{\rho}k(s)^{\lambda_{1}+1}k^{\prime}(s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}b_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|+\Big|\int_{0}^{\rho}sk(s)^{-\lambda_{1}}k^{\prime}(s)^{\frac{1}{2}}a_{1}^{\prime\prime}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq C\ln\rho,

which, with (
4.61
), yields that

|b_{2}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\theta_{2}(1+\frac{1}{2\theta_{1}})}

for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
.
Moreover, by calculating the derivatives explicitly, we obtain

\rho|b_{2}^{\prime}(\rho)|+\rho^{2}|b_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\theta_{2}(1+\frac{1}{2\theta_{1}})}\qquad\,\,\text{for $\rho\geq\rho^{*}$}.

(4.92)

2. For the remainder term
g_{2}(\rho,u)
, recalling from
(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, Proof of Theorem 2.2)
,
g_{2}
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} \displaystyle&\partial_{\rho\rho}g_{2}(\rho,u)-k^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}\partial_{uu}g_{2}(\rho,u)=ub_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho)k(\rho)^{2\lambda_{1}+2}f_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\frac{u}{k(\rho)})+\frac{P^{\prime\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}\partial_{u}g_{1}(\rho,u),\\
\displaystyle&g_{2}(0,u)=0,\quad\partial_{\rho}g_{2}(0,u)=0,\end{aligned}\right.

where
f_{\lambda_{1}}(y)=[1-y^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda_{1}}
.
Similar to the arguments for Lemma
4.8
, we obtain

\displaystyle k^{\prime}(\rho)g_{2}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)k^{\prime}(s)\Big(g_{2}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))+g_{2}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sb_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s)k(s)^{2\lambda_{1}+2}\Big(\int_{u-k(\rho)+k(s)}^{u+k(\rho)-k(s)}yf_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\frac{y}{k(s)})\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}P^{\prime\prime}(s)\Big(g_{1}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))-g_{1}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s,

(4.93)

which yields

\displaystyle\|k^{\prime}(\rho)g_{2}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)\|k^{\prime}(s)g_{2}(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{C}{\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}s|b_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s)|k(s)^{2\lambda_{1}+4}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C}{\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}P^{\prime\prime}(s)\|g_{1}(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)\|k^{\prime}(s)g_{2}(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\mathrm{d}s+I_{1}+I_{2}.

(4.94)

It follows from (
3.2
)–(
3.3
), (
3.6
)–(
3.7
), (
4.90
), (
4.92
),
and Lemma
4.11
that, for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}

\displaystyle|I_{1}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{C}{\rho}\Big(\int_{0}^{\rho_{*}}+\int_{\rho_{*}}^{\rho^{*}}+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}\Big)s|b_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s)|k(s)^{2\lambda_{1}+4}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\rho}\Big(1+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}s^{-\frac{1}{2}+2\theta_{2}+\frac{\theta_{1}}{2\theta_{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq C\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}+2\theta_{2}+\frac{\theta_{1}}{2\theta_{2}}}\leq C\rho^{2\theta_{2}},

(4.95)

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{C}{\rho}\Big(\int_{0}^{\rho_{*}}+\int_{\rho_{*}}^{\rho^{*}}+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}\Big)P^{\prime\prime}(s)\|g_{1}(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\rho}\Big(1+\int_{\rho^{*}}^{\rho}s^{2\theta_{2}-1}\|g_{1}(s,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq\begin{cases}C\rho^{2\theta_{2}}\quad&\text{if }\theta_{2}<\theta_{1},\\
C\rho^{2\theta_{2}}\ln\rho\,\,\,&\text{if }\theta_{2}=\theta_{1}.\end{cases}

(4.96)

Substituting (
4.95
)–(
4.96
) into (
4.94
),
applying Lemma
10.3
and Corollary
10.4
, and using (
3.7
),
we obtain that, for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,

\|g_{2}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\begin{cases}C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\quad&\text{if $\theta_{2}<\theta_{1}$},\\
C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\ln\rho\,\,\,&\text{if $\theta_{2}=\theta_{1}$}.\end{cases}

3. Denoting
\widetilde{f}(s)=sf_{\lambda_{1}+1}(s)
and applying
\partial_{u}
to (
4.93
), we have

\displaystyle k^{\prime}(\rho)\partial_{u}g_{2}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)k^{\prime}(s)\Big(\partial_{u}g_{2}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))+\partial_{u}g_{2}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sb_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s)k(s)^{2\lambda_{1}+3}\Big(\widetilde{f}(\frac{u+k(\rho)-k(s)}{k(s)})-\widetilde{f}(\frac{u-k(\rho)+k(s)}{k(s)})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}P^{\prime\prime}(s)\Big(\partial_{u}g_{1}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))-\partial_{u}g_{1}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s.

(4.97)

Since
|\widetilde{f}(s)|\leq 1
, by similar arguments as in Step 2, we obtain

\|\partial_{u}g_{2}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\qquad\,\text{for $\rho\geq\rho^{*}$}.

4.
Applying
\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}}
to (
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle k^{\prime}(\rho)\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{2}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)k^{\prime}(s)\Big((\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{2})(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))+(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{2})(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sb_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s)k(s)^{\lambda_{1}+3}\Big(\widetilde{f}^{(\lambda_{1})}(\frac{u+k(\rho)-k(s)}{k(s)})-\widetilde{f}^{(\lambda_{1})}(\frac{u-k(\rho)+k(s)}{k(s)})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}P^{\prime\prime}(s)\Big((\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{1})(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))-(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{1})(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
\widetilde{f}^{(\lambda_{1})}:=\partial_{s}^{\lambda_{1}}\widetilde{f}(s)
.
Since
\widetilde{f}^{(\lambda_{1})}
is uniformly bounded, similar arguments as in Step 2 yield

\|\big(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{2}\big)(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\qquad\,\text{for $\rho\geq\rho^{*}$}.

5. Applying
\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+\alpha_{0}}
to (
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle k^{\prime}(\rho)\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{2}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)k^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{2})(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}d(s)k^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{2})(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}sb_{2}^{\prime\prime}(s)k(s)^{\lambda_{1}+3-\alpha_{0}}\Big(\widetilde{f}^{(\lambda_{1})}(\frac{u+k(\rho)-k(s)}{k(s)})-\widetilde{f}^{(\lambda_{1})}(\frac{u-k(\rho)+k(s)}{k(s)})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}P^{\prime\prime}(s)(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}})g_{1}(s,u+k(\rho)-k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\rho}\int_{0}^{\rho}P^{\prime\prime}(s)(\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}})g_{1}(s,u-k(\rho)+k(s))\,\mathrm{d}s.

Noting that
\widetilde{f}^{(\lambda_{1}+\alpha_{0})}(s)
is uniformly bounded, by similar arguments as in Step 2,
we have

\|\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1+\alpha_{0}}g_{2}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\qquad\text{for $\rho\geq\rho^{*}$}.

This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

The following lemma provides the explicit singularities of
\chi(\rho,u-s)
and
(\sigma-u\chi)(\rho,u-s)
.

Lemma 4.14
.

The fractional derivatives
\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}\chi
and
\partial_{u}^{\lambda_{1}+1}(\sigma-u\chi)

admit the expansions
:

\displaystyle\partial_{s}^{\lambda_{1}+1}\chi(\rho,u-s)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\pm}\Big(A_{1,\pm}(\rho)\,\delta(s-u\pm k(\rho))+A_{2,\pm}(\rho)\,H(s-u\pm k(\rho))\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{\pm}\Big(A_{3,\pm}(\rho)\,PV(s-u\pm k(\rho))+A_{4,\pm}(\rho)\,Ci(s-u\pm k(\rho))\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+r_{\chi}(\rho,u-s),

(4.98)

\displaystyle\partial_{s}^{\lambda_{1}+1}(\sigma-u\chi)(\rho,u-s)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\pm}(s-u)\Big(B_{1,\pm}(\rho)\,\delta(s-u\pm k(\rho))+B_{2,\pm}(\rho)\,H(s-u\pm k(\rho))\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{\pm}(s-u)\Big(B_{3,\pm}(\rho)\,PV(s-u\pm k(\rho))+B_{4,\pm}\,Ci(s-u\pm k(\rho))\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{\pm}\Big(B_{5,\pm}(\rho)\,H(s-u\pm k(\rho))+B_{6,\pm}(\rho)\,Ci(s-u\pm k(\rho))\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+r_{\sigma}(\rho,u-s),

(4.99)

where
\delta
is the Dirac measure,
H
is the Heaviside function,
PV
is the principle value distribution,
and
Ci
is the Cosine integral
:

Ci(s):=-\int_{|s|}^{\infty}\frac{\cos y}{y}\,\mathrm{d}y=\log|s|+\int_{0}^{|s|}\frac{\cos y-1}{y}\,\mathrm{d}y+C_{0}\qquad\mbox{for $s\in\mathbb{R}$}

for some constant
C_{0}>0
.
The remainder terms
r_{\chi}
and
r_{\sigma}
are Hölder continuous functions.
Moreover, there exists a positive constant
C=C(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\rho_{*},\rho^{*})
such that, for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1,\pm}^{4}|A_{j,\pm}(\rho)|+\sum\limits_{j=1,\pm}^{6}|B_{j,\pm}|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}},

\displaystyle\|r_{\chi}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{C^{\alpha_{1}}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho,\quad\|r_{\sigma}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{C^{\alpha_{1}}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}},

where
\alpha_{1}\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
is the common Hölder exponent of
r_{\chi}
and
r_{\sigma}
.

Proof.

From
(
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
, Lemma 6.4)
, we obtain (
4.98
)–(
4.99
),
where the coefficients are given by

\displaystyle A_{1,\pm}(\rho)=a_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}}A_{1}^{\lambda_{1}},\quad\,\,\,\,A_{2,\pm}(\rho)=\pm a_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}-1}A_{3}^{\lambda_{1}}+a_{2}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}+1}A_{1}^{\lambda_{1}+1},

\displaystyle A_{3,\pm}(\rho)=\pm a_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}}A_{2}^{\lambda_{1}},\quad A_{4,\pm}(\rho)=\pm a_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}-1}A_{4}^{\lambda_{1}}\pm a_{2}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}+1}A_{2}^{\lambda_{1}+1},

\displaystyle r_{\chi}(\rho,u-s)=a_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}-1}\tilde{q}(\frac{s-u}{k(\rho)})+a_{2}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}+1}\tilde{r}(\frac{s-u}{k(\rho)})-A_{4}^{\lambda_{1}}k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}-1}(\mathrm{log}k(\rho))^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\,+\partial_{s}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{1}(\rho,u-s),

where
A_{i}^{\lambda_{1}},i=1,\cdots,4
, are constants depending only on
\lambda_{1}
,
and
\tilde{r}
and
\tilde{q}
are uniformly bounded Hölder continuous functions.
Thus, using Lemma
4.11
, we see that, for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,

\displaystyle|A_{i,\pm}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}}\rho^{\theta_{2}(\frac{1}{2\theta_{1}}-\frac{1}{2})}\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\theta_{2}}\qquad\,\,\text{for $\,i=1,3$},

\displaystyle|A_{j,\pm}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\theta_{2}}{2}}+C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}-\theta_{2}}\rho^{\theta_{2}(\frac{1}{2\theta_{1}}+\frac{1}{2})}\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\qquad\,\,\text{for $\,j=2,4$}.

\displaystyle\|r_{\chi}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}:=\|r_{\chi}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+[r_{\chi}(\rho,\cdot)]_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}+\rho^{\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}-\frac{3}{2}\theta_{2}}|\ln\rho|^{2}+\rho\big)\leq C\rho.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle B_{1,\pm}(\rho)=b_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}}A_{1}^{\lambda_{1}},\quad B_{2,\pm}(\rho)=\pm b_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}-1}A_{3}^{\lambda_{1}}+b_{2}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}+1}A_{1}^{\lambda_{1}+1},

\displaystyle B_{3,\pm}(\rho)=\pm b_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}}A_{2}^{\lambda_{1}},\quad B_{4,\pm}(\rho)=\pm b_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}-1}A_{4}^{\lambda_{1}}\pm b_{2}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}+1}A_{2}^{\lambda_{1}+1},

\displaystyle B_{5,\pm}(\rho)=(\lambda_{1}+1)b_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}}A_{1}^{\lambda_{1}},\quad B_{6,\pm}(\rho)=\pm(\lambda_{1}+1)b_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}}A_{2}^{\lambda_{1}},

\displaystyle r_{\sigma}(\rho,u-s)

\displaystyle=(s-u)\Big(b_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}-1}\big(-A_{4}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathrm{log}k(\rho))^{2}+\tilde{q}(\frac{s-u}{k(\rho)})\big)+b_{2}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}+1}\tilde{r}(\frac{s-u}{k(\rho)})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+(\lambda_{1}+1)\Big(b_{1}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}}\tilde{r}(\frac{s-u}{k(\rho)})+b_{2}(\rho)k(\rho)^{\lambda_{1}+2}\tilde{\ell}(\frac{s-u}{k(\rho)})\Big)+\partial_{s}^{\lambda_{1}+1}g_{2}(\rho,u-s),

where
\tilde{\ell}
is also a uniformly bounded Hölder continuous function.
Using Lemma
4.13
, we conclude that, for
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,

\displaystyle|B_{i,\pm}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2\theta_{1}}}\rho^{\theta_{2}(\frac{1}{2\theta_{1}}-\frac{1}{2})}\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\qquad\text{for }i=1,3,5,6,

\displaystyle|B_{j,\pm}(\rho)|\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\theta_{2}}{2}}+C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-(\frac{1}{2\theta_{1}}+1)\theta_{2}}\rho^{\theta_{2}(\frac{1}{2\theta_{1}}+\frac{1}{2})}\leq C\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\qquad\text{for }j=2,4,

\displaystyle\|r_{\sigma}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}:=\|r_{\sigma}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+[r_{\sigma}(\rho,\cdot)]_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\big(\rho^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}|\ln\rho|^{2}+\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\big)\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}.

This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

5
Uniform Estimates of Approximate Solutions

As in
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, we construct the approximate solutions via the following approximate
free boundary problem for CNSPEs:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\rho_{t}+(\rho u)_{r}+\frac{2}{r}\rho u=0,\\
&(\rho u)_{t}+(\rho u^{2}+P(\rho))_{r}+\frac{2}{r}\rho u^{2}+\frac{\rho}{r^{2}}\int_{a}^{r}\rho(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y=\varepsilon\Big(\rho(u_{r}+\frac{2}{r}u)\Big)_{r}-\frac{2\varepsilon}{r}\rho_{r}u,\end{aligned}\right.

(5.1)

for
(t,r)\in\Omega_{T}
with

\Omega_{T}=\{(t,r)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\,:\,a\leq r\leq b(t),\;0\leq t\leq T\},

(5.2)

where
\{r=b(t)\,:\,0\leq t\leq T\}
is a free boundary determined by

b^{\prime}(t)=u(t,b(t))\quad\text{for $t>0$},\qquad\,\,\,b(0)=b,

(5.3)

and
a=b^{-1}
with
b\gg 1
. On the free boundary
r=b(t)
, we impose the stress-free boundary condition:

\big(P(\rho)-\varepsilon\rho(u_{r}+\frac{2}{r}u)\big)(t,b(t))=0\qquad\text{for }t>0.

(5.4)

On the fixed boundary
r=a=b^{-1}
, we impose the Dirichlet boundary condition:

u(t,r)|_{r=a}=0\qquad\text{for }t>0.

(5.5)

The initial condition is

(\rho,\rho u)|_{t=0}=(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})\qquad\,\text{for }r\in[a,b].

(5.6)

5.1
Basic estimates

Denote

\displaystyle E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}:=\omega_{3}\int_{a}^{b}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big|u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\big|^{2}+e(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r,\qquad E_{1}^{\varepsilon,b}:=\omega_{3}\varepsilon^{2}\int_{a}^{b}\Big|\big(\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}\big)_{r}\Big|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r.

For given total energy
E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}>0
,
the critical mass
M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}
is defined in (
2.5
)–(
2.8
) by replacing
E_{0}
with
E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}
.

For the approximate initial data
(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon},m_{0}^{\varepsilon})
imposed in (
2.17
)
satisfying (
2.9
)–(
2.10
), using similar arguments
in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, Appendix A)
, we can construct a sequence of smooth
functions
(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})
defined on
[a,b]
, which is compatible with the boundary
conditions (
5.4
)–(
5.5
), such that

(i)

There exists a constant
C_{\varepsilon,b}>0
depending on
(\varepsilon,b)
so that,
for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
b>1
,

0<C_{\varepsilon,b}^{-1}\leq\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(r)\leq C_{\varepsilon,b}<\infty.

(5.7)

(ii)

For all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
b>1
,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(r)r^{2}dr=\frac{M}{\omega_{3}},\qquad E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\leq C(1+E_{0}),\qquad E_{1}^{\varepsilon,b}\leq C(1+M)\varepsilon,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(b)\cong b^{-(3-\alpha)}\qquad\,\,\text{with }\alpha:=\min\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{3(\gamma_{1}-1)}{\gamma_{1}}\}.

(5.9)

(iii)

For each fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, as
b\to\infty
,
(E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},E_{1}^{\varepsilon,b})\rightarrow(E_{0}^{\varepsilon},E_{1}^{\varepsilon})
and

(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})\longrightarrow(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon},m_{0}^{\varepsilon})\qquad\text{in $L^{\tilde{q}}([a,b];r^{2}dr)\times L^{1}([a,b];r^{2}dr)$ for $\tilde{q}\in\{1,\gamma_{2}\}$}.

(5.10)

(iv)

For each fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, there exists a large constant
\mathcal{B}(\varepsilon)>0
such that

M<M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}\qquad\text{for }b\geq\mathcal{B}(\varepsilon)\text{ and }\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{6}{5},\frac{4}{3}],

(5.11)

where

M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}
is defined in (
2.5
)–(
2.8
) by replacing
E_{0}
with
E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}
.

We point out that (
5.9
) is important for us to close the BD-type entropy estimate
in Lemma
5.4
and to obtain the higher integrability of the density in Lemma
5.6
below.

Once the free boundary problem (
5.1
)–(
5.6
) is solved,
we define the potential function
\Phi
to be the solution of the Poisson equation:

\Delta\Phi=\rho\mathbf{I}_{\Omega_{t}},\qquad\lim\limits_{|\mathbf{x}|\to\infty}\Phi(\mathbf{x})=0,

with
\Omega_{t}:=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,:\,a\leq|\mathbf{x}|\leq b(t)\}
,
for which
\rho
has been extended to be zero outside
\Omega_{t}
.
In fact, we can show that
\Phi(t,\mathbf{x})=\Phi(t,r)
with

\Phi_{r}(t,r)=\left\{\begin{aligned} 0\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad&\quad\text{ for }0\leq r\leq a,\\
\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{a}^{r}\rho(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y&\quad\text{ for }a\leq r\leq b(t),\\
\frac{M}{\omega_{3}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\qquad\qquad\quad&\quad\text{ for }r\geq b(t),\end{aligned}\right.

(5.12)

so that
\Phi(t,r)
can be recovered by integrating (
5.12
).

In this section, parameters
(\varepsilon,b)
are fixed with
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
and

b\geq\max\{\rho_{*}^{-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{3}},\mathcal{B}(\varepsilon)\}
such that (
5.11
) holds
and
\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(b)\leq\rho_{*}
.
The global existence of smooth solutions of our approximate problem (
5.1
)–(
5.6
)
whose initial data satisfy (
5.7
)–(
5.11
) and pressure satisfies (
1.4
)–(
1.6
)
can be obtained by using similar arguments in
(
Duan-Li-2015
, §3)
with
\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{4}{3},\infty)
,
or with
\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{6}{5},\frac{4}{3}]
and
M<M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}(\gamma_{2})
,
so the details are omitted here for simplicity.

Noting that the upper and lower bounds of
\rho^{\varepsilon,b}
in
Duan-Li-2015
depend on
parameters
(\varepsilon,b)
, we now establish some uniform estimates, independent of
b
,
such that the limit:
b\to\infty
can be taken to obtain the global weak solutions
of problem (
1.10
) and (
2.17
)–(
2.18
) in §6 below as approximate
solutions of problem (
1.1
) and (
1.13
)–(
1.14
).
Throughout this section, we drop the superscript in both the approximate solutions

(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},u^{\varepsilon,b})(r)
and the approximate initial data
(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})

for simplicity.

For smooth solutions, it is convenient to analyze (
5.1
)–(
5.6
)
in the Lagrangian coordinates. It follows from (
5.3
) that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r=(\rho u)(t,b(t))b(t)^{2}-\int_{a}^{b(t)}(\rho ur^{2})_{r}(t,r)\,\mathrm{d}r=0,

which implies

\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r=\int_{a}^{b}\rho_{0}(r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r=\frac{M}{\omega_{3}}\qquad\,\mbox{for all $t\geq 0$}.

(5.13)

For
r\in[a,b(t)]
and
t\in[0,T]
, the Lagrangian coordinates
(\tau,x)
are defined by

\tau=t,\qquad x(t,r)=\int_{a}^{r}\rho(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y,

which translate
[0,T]\times[a,b(t)]
into a fixed domain
[0,T]\times[0,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}}]
.
By direct calculation, we see that

\nabla_{(t,r)}x=(-\rho ur^{2},\rho r^{2})
,

\nabla_{(t,r)}\tau=(1,0)
,

\nabla_{(\tau,x)}r=(u,\rho^{-1}r^{-2})
,
and
\nabla_{(\tau,x)}t=(1,0)
.
In the Lagrangian coordinates, the initial-boundary value problem (
5.1
)–(
5.6
) becomes

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\rho_{\tau}+\rho^{2}(r^{2}u)_{x}=0,\\
&u_{\tau}+r^{2}P_{x}=-\frac{x}{r^{2}}+\varepsilon r^{2}(\rho^{2}(r^{2}u)_{x})_{x}-2\varepsilon r\rho_{x}u,\end{aligned}\right.

(5.14)

for
(\tau,x)\in[0,T]\times[0,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}}]
, and

u(\tau,0)=0,\quad(P-\varepsilon\rho^{2}(r^{2}u)_{x})(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})=0\qquad\,\,\text{for }\tau\in[0,T],

(5.15)

where
r=r(\tau,x)
is defined by

\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}r(\tau,x)=u(\tau,x)

for
(\tau,x)\in[0,T]\times[0,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}}]
,
and the fixed boundary
x=\frac{M}{\omega_{3}}
corresponds to
the free boundary:
b(\tau)=r(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})
in the Eulerian coordinates.

Lemma 5.1

(Basic energy estimate)
.

The smooth solution
(\rho,u)(t,r)
of problem (
5.1
)–(
5.6
) satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\rho u^{2}+\rho e(\rho)\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\Big(\rho u_{r}^{2}+2\frac{\rho u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s+2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}(\rho u^{2})(s,b(s))b(s)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{a}^{b}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\rho_{0}u_{0}^{2}+\rho_{0}e(\rho_{0})\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho_{0}(t,z)z^{2}dz\Big)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r,

where
\rho(t,r)
has been understood to be
0
for
r\in[0,a]\cup(b(t),\infty)
in the second term
of the left-hand side
(LHS)
and the second term of the right-hand side
(RHS)
.
In particular, there exists a positive constant
C(E_{0},M)
depending only on the total initial energy
E_{0}
and initial-mass
M

such that the following estimates hold for the two separate cases
:

Case 1.
\displaystyle\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{6}{5},\frac{4}{3}]
and
M<M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}
:

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho\Big(\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+e(\rho)\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\rho\Big(u_{r}^{2}+\frac{2u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}(\rho u^{2})(s,b(s))b(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(E_{0},M).

(5.16)

Case 2.
\displaystyle\gamma_{2}>\frac{4}{3}
:

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\frac{1}{2}\rho\Big(u^{2}+e(\rho)\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\rho\Big(u_{r}^{2}+\frac{2u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}(\rho u^{2})(s,b(s))b(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(E_{0},M).

(5.17)

Proof.
We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
Using (
2.3
) and similar calculations as in the proof
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, Lemma 3.1)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho\Big(\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+e(\rho)\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho(t,z)\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\rho\,r\mathrm{d}r

(5.18)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\Big(\rho u_{r}^{2}+2\rho\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s+2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}(\rho u^{2})(s,b(s))\,b(s)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{a}^{b}\rho_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{2}u_{0}^{2}+e\left(\rho_{0}\right)\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\int_{a}^{b}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho_{0}(z)z^{2}\,\mathrm{d}z\Big)\rho_{0}(r)\,r\mathrm{d}r.

2. We now control the second term on the LHS of (
5.18
) and the second term on the RHS of (
5.18
) to close the estimates.
By similar calculations as in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, Lemma 3.1)
, one can obtain

\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\rho\,r\mathrm{d}r=\frac{1}{2\omega_{3}}\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r,

(5.19)

where we have understood
\rho
to be zero for
r\in[0,a)\cup(b(t),\infty)
in (
5.19
).

3. Now we use the internal energy to control the gravitational potential term.
First, we obtain from (
3.12
) that there exist two constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
depending only on
\rho^{*}
such that

\displaystyle\big|\rho e(\rho)-\frac{\kappa_{2}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}}{\gamma_{2}-1}\big|\leq C_{1}\rho^{\max\{\gamma_{2}-\epsilon,0\}}\,\,\,\,\text{for }\rho\geq\rho^{*},\quad\,\,\,\big|\rho e(\rho)-\frac{\kappa_{2}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}}{\gamma_{2}-1}\big|\leq C_{2}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\,\,\,\,\text{for }\rho\leq\rho^{*}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big(\rho e(\rho)-\frac{\kappa_{2}}{\gamma_{2}-1}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\big)\,r^{2}{\rm d}r\Big|

\displaystyle=\int_{\rho(t,r)\geq K}\Big|\rho e(\rho)-\frac{\kappa_{2}}{\gamma_{2}-1}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\Big|\,r^{2}{\rm d}r+\int_{\rho(t,r)\leq K}\Big|\rho e(\rho)-\frac{\kappa_{2}}{\gamma_{2}-1}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\Big|\,r^{2}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}K^{-\min\{\gamma_{2},\epsilon\}}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\,r^{2}{\rm d}r+C_{2}\omega_{3}^{-1}K^{\gamma_{2}-1}\,M,

(5.20)

where
K>\rho^{*}
is some large constant to be chosen later.

Multiplying (
5.11
) by
\Phi
and integrating by parts yield

\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\leq\|\Phi\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\Omega_{t})}\leq\sqrt{A_{3}}\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\Omega_{t})},

(5.21)

where we have used the positive constant
A_{3}:=\frac{4}{3}\omega_{4}^{-\frac{2}{3}}>0
that is
the sharp constant for the Sobolev inequality in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
(see Lemma
10.1
). Then it follows from (
5.19
) and (
5.21
) that

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\rho\,r\mathrm{d}r=\frac{1}{2\omega_{3}}\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{3\omega_{3}}\omega_{4}^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\Omega_{t})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3\omega_{3}}\omega_{4}^{-\frac{2}{3}}\Big(\int_{\Omega_{t}}\rho^{\frac{6(\gamma_{2}-1)}{5\gamma_{2}-6}}\big(\beta\rho+\rho e(\rho)\big)^{-\frac{1}{5\gamma_{2}-6}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\Big(\int_{\Omega_{t}}\big(\beta\rho+\rho e(\rho)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}\omega_{4}^{-\frac{2}{3}}\omega_{3}^{\frac{4-3\gamma_{2}}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\Big(\int_{\Omega_{t}}C_{\max}(\beta)\rho\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big(\beta\rho+\rho e(\rho)\big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}

\displaystyle=B_{\beta}M^{\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big(\beta\rho+\rho e(\rho)\big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}},

(5.22)

where
B_{\beta}
is the constant defined in (
2.8
).

When
\gamma_{2}>\frac{4}{3}
,
i.e.
,
\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}<1
, it follows from (
5.1
)
by taking
\beta=1
that

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\rho\,r\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\geq\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-B_{1}M^{\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\Big(\left(\omega_{3}^{-1}M\right)^{\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}+\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-C(M),

(5.23)

which, with (
5.18
), yields (
5.17
).

When
\gamma_{2}=\frac{4}{3}
,
i.e.
,
\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}=1
. It has been proved in
(
Cheng-Cheng-Lin
, Theorem 3.1)
