that there exists an optimal constant
C_{\min}=6\kappa_{2}M_{\rm ch}^{-\frac{3}{2}}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\,\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\rho\,r\mathrm{d}r=\frac{1}{2\omega_{3}}\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\min}}{2\omega_{3}}\|\rho\|_{L^{1}(\Omega_{t})}^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}(\Omega_{t})}^{\frac{4}{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\min}}{2}M^{\frac{2}{3}}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho^{\frac{4}{3}}\,r^{2}{\rm d}r,

(5.24)

which, with (
5.1
), yields

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\rho\,r\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\geq\Big(3\kappa_{2}-\frac{C_{\min}}{2}M^{\frac{2}{3}}-C_{1}K^{-\min\{\gamma_{2},\epsilon\}}\Big)\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho^{\frac{4}{3}}\,r^{2}{\rm d}r-C(M,K).

(5.25)

Since
M<M_{\rm ch}
, we can always choose
K>\rho^{*}
large enough such that

3\kappa_{2}-\frac{C_{\min}}{2}M^{\frac{2}{3}}-C_{1}K^{-\min\{\gamma_{2},\epsilon\}}>0.

Then one can deduce (
5.16
) for
\gamma_{2}=\frac{4}{3}
from (
5.18
), (
5.25
), and the fact that
\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\geq C\rho e(\rho)
.

When
\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{6}{5},\frac{4}{3})
, we define

F(s;\beta)=s-B_{\beta}M^{\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\left(\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta M+s\right)^{\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\qquad\,\,\text{for }s\geq 0\text{ and any fixed }\beta>0.

A direct calculation shows that

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{\mathrm{d}F(s;\beta)}{\mathrm{d}s}=1-\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}B_{\beta}M^{\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\big(\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta M+s\big)^{\frac{4-3\gamma_{2}}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}},\\
&\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}F(s;\beta)}{\mathrm{d}s^{2}}=-\frac{4-3\gamma_{2}}{9(\gamma_{2}-1)^{2}}B_{\beta}M^{\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\big(\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta M+s\big)^{\frac{7-6\gamma_{2}}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}},\end{aligned}\right.

which yields that
\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}F(s;\beta)}{\mathrm{d}s^{2}}<0
for
s>0
since
\gamma_{2}<\frac{4}{3}
.
Thus,
F(s;\beta)
is concave with respect to
s>0
.
We denote

s_{*}(\beta)=\Big(\frac{B_{\beta}}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}\Big)^{-\frac{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}{4-3\gamma_{2}}}M^{-\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{4-3\gamma_{2}}}-\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta M,

(5.26)

which is the critical point of
F(s)
satisfying
\frac{\mathrm{d}F(s;\beta)}{\mathrm{d}s}(s_{*}(\beta))=0
.
The maximum of
F(s;\beta)
with respect to
s>0
is

F(s_{*}(\beta);\beta)=(4-3\gamma_{2})\Big(\frac{B_{\beta}}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}\Big)^{-\frac{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}{4-3\gamma_{2}}}M^{-\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{4-3\gamma_{2}}}-\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta M.

(5.27)

It follows from the definition of
M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}
that, if
M<M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}
,
there exists
\beta_{0}>0
such that
M<M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}(\beta_{0})
. Then, from (
5.26
)–(
5.27
), we have

\displaystyle F(s_{*}(\beta_{0});\beta_{0})>\frac{E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\omega_{3}},

(5.28)

\displaystyle s_{*}(\beta_{0})>\Big(\frac{B_{\beta_{0}}}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}\Big)^{-\frac{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}{4-3\gamma_{2}}}\big(M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}(\beta_{0})\big)^{-\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{4-3\gamma_{2}}}-\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta_{0}M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}(\beta_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,\,=\frac{1}{4-3\gamma_{2}}\big(E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}+\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta_{0}M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}(\beta_{0})\big)-\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta_{0}M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b}(\beta_{0})>\frac{E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\omega_{3}},

(5.29)

where we have used that
\frac{1}{4-3\gamma_{2}}>\frac{5}{2}>1

for
\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{6}{5},\frac{4}{3})
.
Then, combining (
5.18
) and (
5.1
) with (
5.28
)–(
5.29
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &F(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r;\beta_{0})\leq\frac{E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\omega_{3}}<F(s_{*}(\beta_{0});\beta_{0}),\\
&\int_{a}^{b}\big(\rho_{0}e(\rho_{0})\big)(r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\leq\frac{E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\omega_{3}}<s_{*}(\beta_{0}).\end{aligned}

(5.30)

Hence, due to the continuity of
\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big(\rho e(\rho)\big)(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r

with respect to
t
, the strict inequality:

\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big(\rho e(\rho)\big)(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r<s_{*}(\beta_{0})

(5.31)

must hold. Otherwise, there exists
t_{0}>0
such that

\int_{a}^{b(t_{0})}\big(\rho e(\rho)\big)(t_{0},r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r=s_{*}(\beta_{0})
, which yields

F(\int_{a}^{b(t_{0})}(\rho e(\rho))(t_{0},r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r;\beta_{0})=F(s_{*}(\beta_{0});\beta_{0})>\frac{E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\omega_{3}}.

This contradicts (
5.30
).
Thus, we prove (
5.31
) under condition (
5.11
).

Therefore, under condition (
5.11
), it follows from (
5.26
) and (
5.31
) that

\displaystyle F(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r;\beta_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad-B_{\beta_{0}}M^{\frac{5\gamma_{2}-6}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\big(s_{*}(\beta_{0})+\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta_{0}M\big)^{\frac{4-3\gamma_{2}}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta_{0}M\Big)

\displaystyle=(4-3\gamma_{2})\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-3(\gamma_{2}-1)\omega_{3}^{-1}\beta_{0}M^{\frac{5}{3}}.

(5.32)

Combining (
5.18
) and (
5.25
) with (
5.1
), we conclude (
5.16
).

\hfill\square

Corollary 5.2
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
5.1
and noting (
3.5
),

\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\leq C\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big(\rho+\rho e(\rho)\big)(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\text{ for $t\geq 0$}.

Corollary 5.3
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
5.1
,
it follows from (
5.12
), (
5.16
)–(
5.17
), and (
5.19
) that,
for
t\geq 0
and
r\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left|r^{2}\Phi_{r}(t,r)\right|\leq\frac{M}{\omega_{3}},

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)\rho(t,r)\,r\mathrm{d}r+\|\Phi(t)\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla\Phi(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C(M,E_{0}).

For later use, we analyze the boundary value of density
\rho
.
Using
\eqref{2.13}_{1}
and (
5.15
), we have

\rho_{\tau}(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\,P(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})\leq 0,

(5.33)

which yields that
\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})\leq\rho_{0}(\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})
.
In the Eulerian coordinates, it is equivalent to

\rho(t,b(t))\leq\rho_{0}(b).

(5.34)

Moreover, noting (
5.8
) and
b\geq(\rho_{*})^{-\gamma_{1}/3}
,
we see that
\rho(t,b(t))\leq\rho_{0}(b)\leq\rho_{*}
for all
t\geq 0
.
From
\eqref{A.1-1}_{1}
and (
5.33
), there exists a positive constant
\tilde{C}

depending only on
(\gamma_{1},\kappa_{1})
such that

\rho_{\tau}(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\,P(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})\geq-\frac{\tilde{C}}{\varepsilon}\big(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})\big)^{\gamma_{1}}
,
which implies

\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})\geq\rho_{0}(\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})\Big(1+\frac{\tilde{C}(\gamma_{1}-1)}{\varepsilon}\big(\rho_{0}(\frac{M}{\omega_{3}})\big)^{\gamma_{1}-1}\tau\Big)^{-\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}-1}}.

Therefore, in the Eulerian coordinates,

\rho(t,b(t))\geq\rho_{0}(b)\Big(1+\frac{\tilde{C}(\gamma_{1}-1)}{\varepsilon}(\rho_{0}(b))^{\gamma_{1}-1}t\Big)^{-\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}-1}}\qquad\mbox{for $t\geq 0$}.

(5.35)

Lemma 5.4

(BD-type entropy estimate)
.

Under the conditions of
Lemma
5.1
, for any given
T>0
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big|\left(\sqrt{\rho}\right)_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{3}\,P(\rho(t,b(t)))\,b(t)^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{3\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\big(P(\rho)P^{\prime}(\rho)\big)(s,b(s))\,b(s)^{3}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(E_{0},M,T)\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $t\in[0,T]$}.

(5.36)

Proof.
We divide the proof into three steps.

1. Using (
2.3
) and similar calculations as in the proof
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, Lemma 3.3)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big(u+\varepsilon\frac{\rho_{r}}{\rho}\big)^{2}\rho+\rho e(\rho)\Big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)\rho\,r\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}\rho_{r}^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{3}P(\rho(t,b(t)))\,b(t)^{3}+\frac{1}{3\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\big(P(\rho)P^{\prime}(\rho)\big)(s,b(s))\,b(s)^{3}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{a}^{b}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big(u_{0}+\varepsilon\frac{\rho_{0,r}}{\rho}\big)^{2}+e(\rho_{0})\Big)\rho_{0}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\int_{a}^{b}\Big(\int_{a}^{r}\rho_{0}(y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)\rho_{0}(r)\,r\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{3}P(\rho_{0}\left(b\right))b^{3}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\rho^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s-\frac{M\varepsilon}{\omega_{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\rho(s,b(s))\,\mathrm{d}s,

which, with Lemma
5.1
, yields

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{a}^{b(t)}|\left(\sqrt{\rho}\right)_{r}|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{3}P(\rho(t,b(t)))\,b(t)^{3}+\frac{1}{3\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\big(P(\rho)P^{\prime}(\rho)\big)(s,b(s))\,b(s)^{3}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(E_{0},M)+\frac{1}{3}P(\rho_{0}(b))b^{3}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\rho^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s-\frac{M\varepsilon}{\omega_{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\rho(s,b(s))\,\mathrm{d}s.

(5.37)

2. For the second term on the RHS of (
5.37
), it follows from (
5.8
) and
\eqref{A.1-1}_{1}
that

\frac{1}{3}P(\rho_{0}(b))b^{3}\leq C.

(5.38)

For the last term on the RHS of (
5.37
), using (
5.34
), we have

\Big|\frac{M\varepsilon}{\omega_{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\rho(s,b(s))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq C(M)\rho_{0}(b)T\leq C(M,T).

(5.39)

3. To close the estimates, we need to control the third term on the RHS of (
5.37
), that is,

\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\rho^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s=\frac{\varepsilon}{\omega_{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\rho(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{s})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

We divide the estimate of the above term into the following two cases:

Case 1. For
\gamma_{2}\geq 2
, it follows from Corollary
5.2
that

\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\rho^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}(\rho+\rho^{\gamma_{2}})\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\leq C(E_{0},M,T).

(5.40)

Case 2. For
\displaystyle\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{6}{5},2)
, then
3\gamma_{2}>2
. A direct calculation shows that

\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\rho^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\{\rho\leq 2\rho^{*}\}}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\leq 2\varepsilon\rho^{*}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\rho\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\leq C(M,\rho^{*}).

(5.41)

Denote
\sqrt{F(\rho)}:=\int_{0}^{\rho}\sqrt{\frac{P^{\prime}(s)}{s}}\,\mathrm{d}s
. Then it follows from
\eqref{A.2-1}_{2}
that

\displaystyle\sqrt{F(\rho)}

\displaystyle\geq\big(1-2^{-\frac{\gamma_{2}}{2}}\big)\frac{2\sqrt{(1-\mathfrak{a}_{0})\kappa_{2}\gamma_{2}}}{\gamma_{2}}\rho^{\frac{\gamma_{2}}{2}}:=C(\gamma_{2})^{-\frac{\gamma_{2}}{2\overline{\vartheta}}}\rho^{\frac{\gamma_{2}}{2}}\qquad\text{for }\rho\in[2\rho^{*},\infty),

which, with Corollary
5.2
, implies that, for

\overline{\vartheta}=\frac{3(2-\gamma_{2})}{4}
,

\displaystyle\|\rho\mathbf{I}_{\{\rho\geq 2\rho^{*}\}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho\mathbf{I}_{\{\rho\geq 2\rho^{*}\}}\|_{L^{3\gamma_{2}}(\Omega_{t})}^{\overline{\vartheta}}\|\rho\mathbf{I}_{\{\rho\geq 2\rho^{*}\}}\|_{L^{\gamma_{2}}(\Omega_{t})}^{1-\overline{\vartheta}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\gamma_{2})\|\sqrt{F(\rho)}\|_{L^{6}(\Omega_{t})}^{\frac{2\overline{\vartheta}}{\gamma_{2}}}\|\rho\|_{L^{\gamma_{2}}(\Omega_{t})}^{1-\overline{\vartheta}}.

(5.42)

For
B_{R}(\textbf{0})\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the following Sobolev’s inequality holds:

\|f\|_{L^{6}(B_{R}(\mathbf{0}))}\leq C\big(\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}(\mathbf{0}))}+R^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}(\mathbf{0}))}\big).

(5.43)

It follows from (
5.13
) and Corollary
5.2
that

\displaystyle\frac{M}{\omega_{3}}

\displaystyle=\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\leq\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}}\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq Cb(t)^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}{\gamma_{2}}}\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}},

which yields

b(t)^{-1}\leq CM^{-\frac{\gamma_{2}}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{3(\gamma_{2}-1)}}\leq C\left(M,E_{0}\right).

(5.44)

Using
\eqref{A.1-1}_{2}
–
\eqref{A.2-1}_{2}
leads to
F(\rho)\leq C(\rho+\rho^{\gamma_{2}})
,
which, with (
5.43
)–(
5.44
) and Corollary
5.2
,
implies

\displaystyle\big\|\sqrt{F(\rho)}\big\|_{L^{6}(\Omega_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\big\|\nabla(\sqrt{F(\rho)})\big\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}+b(t)^{-1}\big\|\sqrt{F(\rho)}\big\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(M,E_{0})\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}F(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0})\Big(1+\big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big).

(5.45)

Substituting (
5.45
) into (
5.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\rho^{2}r^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\{\rho\geq 2\rho^{*}\}}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0},T)\varepsilon\Big(1+\big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{2\overline{\vartheta}}{\gamma_{2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0},T)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s,

(5.46)

where we have used

\frac{2\bar{\vartheta}}{\gamma_{2}}\in(0,1)
for
\gamma_{2}>\frac{6}{5}
.
Finally, substituting (
5.38
)–(
5.41
) and (
5.46
) into (
5.37
),
we conclude (
5.36
).

\hfill\Box

In order to take the limit:
b\to\infty
, we need to make sure that domain
\Omega_{T}

can be expanded to
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
for fixed
\varepsilon>0
:
\lim\limits_{b\to\infty}b(t)=\infty
.

Lemma 5.5

(Expanding of domain
\Omega_{T}
)
.

Given
T>0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, there exists

C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon)>0
such that, if
b\geq C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon)
,

b(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}b\qquad\,\,\text{for $t\in[0,T]$}.

(5.47)

Proof.

Noting
b(0)=b

and the continuity of
b(t)
,
we first make the
a priori
assumption:

b(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}b.

(5.48)

Integrating (
5.3
) over
[0,t]
yields

b(t)=b+\int_{0}^{t}u(s,b(s))\,\mathrm{d}s.

(5.49)

It follows from (
5.35
), (
5.48
), and Lemma
5.1
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|u(s,b(s))|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon(\rho u^{2}r)(s,b(s))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\rho(s,b(s))b(s)}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0})\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(1+\tilde{C}(\gamma_{1}-1)\varepsilon^{-1}\rho_{*}^{\gamma_{1}-1}s)^{\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}-1}}}{\rho_{0}(b)b}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0},T,\rho_{*},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\varepsilon)\rho_{0}(b)^{-\frac{1}{2}}b^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.50)

We take

C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon):=\max\big\{\rho_{*}^{-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{3}},(4C(M,E_{0},T,\rho_{*},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\varepsilon))^{\frac{2}{\alpha}},\mathcal{B}(\varepsilon)\big\},

which, with (
5.8
) and (
5.50
), implies that

\Big|\int_{0}^{t}u(s,b(s))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq\int_{0}^{t}|u(s,b(s))|\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{1}{4}b,

(5.51)

provided that
b\geq C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon)
. Combining (
5.51
) with (
5.49
), we have

b(t)\geq\frac{3}{4}b.

(5.52)

Thus, we have closed the
a priori
assumption (
5.48
).
Finally, using (
5.52
) and the continuity argument, we can conclude (
5.47
).

\hfill\Box

5.2
Higher integrability of the density and the velocity

As implied in
Chen-Perepelitsa-2010
, the higher integrabilities of
the density and the velocity are important for the
L^{p}
compensated compactness framework.
However, for the general pressure law,
due to the lack of an explicit formula for the entropy kernel,
for the special entropy pair
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
by taking the test function

\psi=\frac{1}{2}s|s|
in (
2.15
)–(
2.16
),
we can not obtain that
q^{\psi}\gtrsim\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}
in general.
To derive the higher integrability of the velocity,
we use the special entropy pair constructed in Lemma
4.1
,
at the cost of the higher integrability of the density over domain
[0,T]\times[d,b(t)]

for some
d>0
. Since
b(t)\to\infty
as
b\to\infty
,
we indeed need the higher integrability of the density on the unbounded domain.
We point out that this is different from the case of
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021

in which only the higher integrability on the bounded domain
[0,T]\times[d,D]
for any given
0<d<D<\infty

is needed.

Lemma 5.6

(Higher integrability on the density)
.

Let
(\rho,u)
be a smooth solution of (
5.1
)–(
5.6
).
Then, under the assumption of
Lemma
5.1
, for any given
d>2b^{-1}>0
,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\rho P(\rho)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T).

(5.53)

Proof.

Let
\omega(r)
be a smooth function with
\operatorname{supp}\omega\subset(\frac{d}{2},\infty)

and
\omega(r)=1
for
r\in[d,\infty)
. Multiplying
\eqref{2.1}_{2}
by
w(y)y^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle(y^{2}\rho u\omega)_{t}+(y^{2}\rho u^{2}\omega)_{y}+(y^{2}P(\rho)\omega)_{y}-\omega_{y}\big(y^{2}\rho u^{2}+y^{2}P(\rho)\big)+\rho\omega\int_{a}^{r}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=2yP(\rho)\omega+\varepsilon(y^{2}\rho u_{y}\omega)_{y}-\varepsilon\omega_{y}y^{2}\rho u_{y}-2\varepsilon\rho u\,\omega.

(5.54)

Integrating (
5.54
) with respect to
y
from
\frac{d}{2}
to
r
and then multiplying
the equation
by
\rho(t,r)
yield

\displaystyle r^{2}\rho(t,r)P(\rho(t,r))\omega(r)

\displaystyle=-\rho\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\,\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y-r^{2}\rho^{2}u^{2}\omega(r)+\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\omega_{y}\rho u\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\omega_{y}P(\rho)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y+2\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}P(\rho)\,\omega\,y\mathrm{d}y-\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho\omega\Big(\int_{a}^{y}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon r^{2}\rho^{2}u_{r}\omega(r)-\varepsilon\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\omega_{y}\rho u_{y}\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y-2\varepsilon\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\,\omega\,\mathrm{d}y.

(5.55)

Using
\eqref{2.1}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\rho\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\Big(\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)_{t}+\Big(\rho u\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)_{r}

\displaystyle\quad-\rho^{2}u^{2}\omega(r)r^{2}+\frac{2}{r}\rho u\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y,

which, with (
5.55
), yields that

\displaystyle r^{2}\rho(t,r)P(\rho(t,r))\omega(r)

\displaystyle=-\Big(\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)_{t}-\Big(\rho u\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)_{r}-\frac{2}{r}\rho u\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y+\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\omega_{y}\rho u^{2}\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\omega_{y}P(\rho)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y+2\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}P(\rho)\omega\,y\mathrm{d}y+\varepsilon\rho^{2}u_{r}\omega(r)r^{2}-\varepsilon\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u_{y}\omega_{y}\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-2\varepsilon\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,\mathrm{d}y-\rho\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho\omega\,\Big(\int_{a}^{y}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\,\mathrm{d}y.

(5.56)

Multiplying (
5.56
) by
\omega(r)
leads to

\displaystyle r^{2}\rho(t,r)P(\rho(t,r))\omega^{2}(r)

\displaystyle=-\Big(\rho\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega(y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)_{t}-\Big(\rho u\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega(y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)_{r}+\omega_{r}\rho u\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2}{r}\rho u\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y+\rho\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega_{y}\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y+\rho\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}P(\rho)\,\omega_{y}\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+2\rho\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}P(\rho)\omega\,y\mathrm{d}y-\rho\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho\omega\Big(\int_{a}^{y}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\,\mathrm{d}y-\varepsilon\rho\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u_{y}\,\omega_{y}\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-2\varepsilon\rho\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\,\omega\,\mathrm{d}y+\varepsilon r^{2}\rho^{2}u_{r}\omega^{2}(r):=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{11}I_{i}.

(5.57)

Using Lemma
5.1
and (
5.13
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\big((\rho u+P(\rho))\omega(y)+\varepsilon\rho u_{y}\omega_{y}\big)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big(\rho u^{2}+\rho+\rho^{\gamma_{2}}\big)\omega(y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y+\varepsilon\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho(u_{y}^{2}+1)|\omega_{y}|\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\leq C(M,E_{0},\|\omega\|_{C^{1}}),

which yields

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}I_{i}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big|\leq C(M,E_{0},T,\|\omega\|_{C^{1}})(d^{-2}+d^{-4})\quad\,\,\text{for $i=3,4,\cdots,10$},

(5.58)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}I_{1}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big|=\Big|\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho(T,r)\omega(r)\Big(\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}y^{2}\rho(T,y)u(T,y)\omega(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)\mathrm{d}r\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big|\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho(0,r)\omega(r)\Big(\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}y^{2}\rho(0,y)u(0,y)\omega(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)\mathrm{d}r\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\leq C(M,E_{0},\|\omega\|_{C^{1}})d^{-2}.

(5.59)

For
I_{2}
, using (
5.8
), (
5.34
), (
5.51
), and
b\gg 1
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}I_{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\Big(\rho u\omega(r)\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{r}\rho u\omega(y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{0}^{T}(\rho u)(t,b(t))\Big(\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho u\omega(y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(E_{0},M)b^{-3+\frac{1}{2}}b\leq C(E_{0},M).

(5.60)

For
I_{11}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}I_{11}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho^{2}u_{r}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho^{3}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t+C(M,E_{0},T,\|\omega\|_{C^{1}}).

(5.61)

We divide the estimate of

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\rho^{3}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t
into two cases:

Case 1.
\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{6}{5},2)
: For

t\in[0,T]
,
denoting

A(t):=\{r\in[\frac{d}{2},b(t)]\,:\,\rho(t,r)\geq\rho^{*}\},

then it follows from (
5.13
) that
|A(t)|\leq C(d,\rho^{*})M
.
For any
r\in A(t)
, let
r_{0}
be the closest point to
r
so that

\rho(t,r_{0})=\rho^{*}
with
|r-r_{0}|\leq|A(t)|\leq C(d,\rho^{*})M
.
Then, for any smooth function
f(\rho)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in A(t)}\big(f(\rho(t,r))\omega^{2}(r)\big)

\displaystyle\leq f(\rho(t,r_{0}))\omega^{2}(r_{0})+\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\partial_{y}\big(f(\rho(t,y))\omega^{2}(y)\big)\,\mathrm{d}y\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\omega\|_{C^{1}})|f(\rho^{*})|+\int_{A(t)}\big|\partial_{y}\big(f(\rho(t,y))\omega^{2}(y)\big)\big|\,\mathrm{d}y.

Recalling (
3.3
) and (
3.5
),
we notice that
P(\rho)\cong\rho^{\gamma_{2}}
and
e(\rho)\cong\rho^{\gamma_{2}-1}
for any
r\in A(t)
.
Then

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho^{3}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho^{3}{\bf I}_{\{\rho\leq\rho^{\ast}\}}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho^{3}{\bf I}_{\{\rho\geq\rho^{\ast}\}}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0},\rho^{*},T)+C(M,E_{0})\,\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho e(\rho)\,r^{2}dr\Big)\sup_{r\in A(t)}\Big(\frac{\rho^{2}}{e(\rho)}\omega^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0},\rho^{*},T)+C(M,E_{0})\,\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\Big|\Big(\frac{\rho^{2}}{e(\rho)}\omega^{2}\Big)_{r}(t,r)\Big|\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0})\,\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\Big(\big(\frac{2\rho}{e(\rho)}-\frac{P(\rho)}{e(\rho)^{2}}\big)|\rho_{r}|\omega^{2}+\frac{\rho^{2}}{e(\rho)}\omega|\omega_{r}|\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+C(M,E_{0},\rho^{*},T).

(5.62)

A direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\varepsilon\Big(\frac{2\rho}{e(\rho)}-\frac{P(\rho)}{e(\rho)^{2}}\Big)|\rho_{r}|\,\omega^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\varepsilon\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\varepsilon\Big(\frac{2\rho}{e(\rho)}-\frac{P(\rho)}{e(\rho)^{2}}\Big)^{2}\frac{\rho}{P^{\prime}(\rho)}omega^{2}\,r^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0},T)+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\varepsilon\rho^{6-3\gamma_{2}}\omega^{2}\,r^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0},T)+\varepsilon C(M,E_{0})^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\rho^{3}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+C(M,E_{0})\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}(r^{2})^{-\frac{3-\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}-1}}\omega^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0},T)+\varepsilon C(M,E_{0})^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho^{3}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t.

(5.63)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\varepsilon\frac{\rho^{2}}{e(\rho)}\omega|\omega_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\varepsilon\sup_{r\in A(t)}(\rho\omega)(t,r)\int_{A(t)}\frac{\rho}{e(\rho)}|\omega_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho^{*})\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\varepsilon\sup_{r\in A_{t}}(\rho\omega)(t,r)\int_{A(t)}\rho|\omega_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho^{*},M,\|\omega\|_{C^{1}})d^{-2}\int_{0}^{T}\,\varepsilon\sup_{r\in A(t)}(\rho\omega)(t,r)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho^{*},M,\|\omega\|_{C^{1}},T)d^{-2}+C(\rho^{*},M,\|\omega\|_{C^{1}})d^{-2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\varepsilon\big(|\rho_{r}|\omega+\rho|\omega_{r}|\big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho^{*},M,\|\omega\|_{C^{1}},T)d^{-2}\Big(1+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A(t)}\varepsilon\Big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}|\rho_{r}|^{2}\omega+\rho|\omega_{r}|+\rho^{2-\gamma_{2}}\omega\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho^{*},M,\|\omega\|_{C^{1}},T)d^{-2}+C(\rho^{*},M,E_{0},T,\|\omega\|_{C^{1}})d^{-4}.

(5.64)

Combining (
5.2
)–(
5.64
), we obtain that, for
\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{6}{5},2)
,

\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho^{3}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(M,E_{0},\rho^{*},T,\|\omega\|_{C^{1}})(1+d^{-4}).

(5.65)

Case 2.
\gamma_{2}\in[2,3)
: Using (
5.13
) and the same argument as for (
5.64
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\frac{d}{2}}^{b(t)}\rho^{3}\omega^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M)\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon\sup_{r\in[\frac{d}{2},b(t)]}(\rho^{2}\omega)(t,r)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M,\rho^{*},\|\omega\|_{C^{1}},T)+C(M)\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon\sup_{r\in A(t)}(\rho^{2}\omega)(t,r)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(M,\rho^{*},\|\omega\|_{C^{1}},T)+C(M,E_{0},\rho^{*},\|\omega\|_{C^{1}},T)d^{-2}.

(5.66)

Finally, integrating (
5.57
) over
[0,T]\times[\frac{d}{2},b(t)]
and using (
5.58
)–(
5.2
)
and (
5.65
)–(
5.2
), we conclude (
5.53
).

\hfill\Box

Corollary 5.7
.

It follows from (
3.3
) and
Lemma
5.6
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\rho^{\gamma_{2}+1}(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\big(\rho+\rho P(\rho)\big)(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T).

(5.67)

In order to use the
L^{p}
compensated compactness framework,
we still need to obtain the higher integrability of the velocity (see
Chen-Perepelitsa-2010
).
With the help of Lemma
5.6
, we use the special entropy pair constructed
in Lemma
4.1
to achieve this.

Lemma 5.8

(Higher integrability of the velocity)
.

Let
(\rho,u)
be the smooth solution of (
5.1
)–(
5.6
).
Then, under the assumption of
Lemma
5.1
,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}(\rho|u|^{3})(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(d,D,\rho^{*},M,E_{0},T)\qquad\,\mbox{for any $(d,D)\Subset[a,b(t)]$}.

Proof.

Considering
\eqref{2.1}_{1}\times\hat{\eta}_{\rho}r^{2}+\eqref{2.1}_{2}\times\hat{\eta}_{m}r^{2}
, we can obtain

\displaystyle(\hat{\eta}r^{2})_{t}+(\hat{q}r^{2})_{r}+2r\big(-\hat{q}+\rho u\hat{\eta}_{\rho}+\rho u^{2}\hat{\eta}_{m}\big)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\,r^{2}\Big((\rho u_{r})_{r}+2\rho\big(\frac{u}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)\hat{\eta}_{m}-\rho\int_{a}^{r}\rho\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\,\hat{\eta}_{m}.

(5.68)

Using (
5.3
), a direct calculation yields

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{r}^{b(t)}\hat{\eta}\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y=(u\hat{\eta})(t,b(t))\,b(t)^{2}+\int_{r}^{b(t)}\partial_{t}\hat{\eta}(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y.

(5.69)

Integrating (
5.68
) over
[r,b(t))
and using (
5.69
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{q}(t,r)\,r^{2}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,y)(\rho u_{y}y^{2})_{y}\,\mathrm{d}y+2\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,y)\,\rho u\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad\,+\Big(\int_{r}^{b(t)}\hat{\eta}(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)_{t}+(\hat{q}-u\hat{\eta})(t,b(t))\,b(t)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\,+2\int_{r}^{b(t)}\big(-\hat{q}+\rho u\hat{\eta}_{\rho}+\rho u^{2}\hat{\eta}_{m}\big)\,y\mathrm{d}y+\int_{r}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{y}\rho\,z^{2}{\rm d}z\Big)\rho\,\hat{\eta}_{m}\,\mathrm{d}y.

(5.70)

We now control the terms on the RHS of (
5.70
).
For the third term on the RHS of (
5.70
), it follows from
(
5.9
)
, (
5.34
), (
5.44
),
and Lemmas
4.1
,
5.1
, and
5.4
–
5.5
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\big|(\hat{q}-u\hat{\eta})(t,b(t))\big|b(t)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\left((\rho(t,b(t)))^{\gamma_{1}+\theta_{1}}+(\rho^{\gamma_{1}}|u|)(t,b(t))\right)b(t)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon(\rho|u|^{2})(t,b(t))b(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\rho(t,b(t)))^{2\gamma_{1}-1}b(t)^{3}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(M,E_{0},T)\int_{0}^{T}(\rho(t,b(t)))^{\theta_{1}}b(t)^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C(M,E_{0},T).

(5.71)

For the first term on the RHS of (
5.70
), integrating by parts yields

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,y)\,(\rho u_{y}y^{2})_{y}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,b(t))\,(\rho u_{r})(t,b(t))\,b(t)^{2}-\varepsilon\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,r)\,(\rho u_{r})(t,r)\,r^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\rho u_{y}\big(\hat{\eta}_{mu}u_{y}+\hat{\eta}_{m\rho}\rho_{y}\big)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y.

(5.72)

It follows from (
5.4
) and Lemma
4.1
that

\displaystyle|\varepsilon\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,b(t))\,(\rho u_{r})(t,b(t))\,b(t)^{2}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,b(t))\Big(\varepsilon\rho\big(u_{r}+\frac{2}{r}u\big)(t,b(t))-2\varepsilon b(t)^{-1}\,(\rho u)(t,b(t))\Big)b(t)^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,b(t))\,p(\rho)(t,b(t))\,b(t)^{2}-2\varepsilon\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,b(t))\,(\rho u)(t,b(t))\,b(t)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\big((\rho^{\gamma_{1}}|u|)(t,b(t))+(\rho(t,b(t)))^{\gamma_{1}+\theta_{1}}\big)b(t)^{2}+C\varepsilon\big((\rho|u|^{2})(t,b(t))+(\rho(t,b(t)))^{\gamma_{1}}\big)b(t).

which,
with similar arguments as in (
5.2
), yields

\int_{0}^{T}\left|\varepsilon\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,b(t))\,(\rho u_{r})(t,b(t))\,b^{2}(t)\right|\mathrm{d}t\leq C(M,E_{0},T).

(5.73)

Hence, using (
5.36
), (
5.2
)–(
5.73
), and Lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\hat{\eta}_{m}(\rho u_{y}y^{2})_{y}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big|\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}|\varepsilon\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,b(t))\,(\rho u_{r})(t,b(t))|\,b(t)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}|\varepsilon\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,r)\,(\rho u_{r})(t,r)|\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\int_{r}^{b(t)}\rho|u_{y}|\big(\hat{\eta}_{mu}|u_{y}|+\hat{\eta}_{m\rho}|\rho_{y}|\big)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big|\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(D,M,E_{0},T)+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon|\rho u_{r}|\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta(\rho)}\big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+C(D)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\rho|u_{y}|\big(|u_{y}|+\rho^{\theta(\rho)-1}|\rho_{y}|\big)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(D,M,E_{0},T)+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\varepsilon\big(\rho|u|^{2}+\rho|u_{r}|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma(\rho)}\big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+C(D)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\rho|u_{y}|^{2}\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}t+C(D)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\rho^{\gamma(\rho)-2}|\rho_{y}|^{2}\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(D,M,E_{0},T).

(5.74)

For the second term, third term, and sixth term on the RHS of (
5.70
), using (
3.4
)–(
3.5
)
and Lemmas
4.1
and
5.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big(2\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\hat{\eta}_{m}(t,y)\rho u\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(d)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta(\rho)}\big)\,\rho|u|\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\varepsilon\big(\rho|u|^{2}+\rho+\rho e(\rho)\big)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T),

(5.75)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big(\int_{r}^{b(t)}\hat{\eta}(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)_{t}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b(t)}|\hat{\eta}(T,y)|\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}r+\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b}|\hat{\eta}(0,y)|\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b(t)}\big(\rho^{\gamma(\rho)}+\rho|u|^{2}\big)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{d}^{D}\int_{r}^{b(t)}\big(\rho e(\rho)+\rho+\rho|u|^{2}\big)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}r\leq C(D,M,E_{0},T),

(5.76)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big(\int_{r}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{y}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\rho\,\hat{\eta}_{m}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\int_{r}^{b(t)}\Big(\int_{a}^{y}\rho\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z\Big)\rho\,\hat{\eta}_{m}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big|\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\rho\big(|u|+\rho^{\gamma(\rho)}\big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\big(\rho|u|^{2}+\rho+\rho e(\rho)\big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T).

(5.77)

For the fifth term on the RHS of (
5.70
), we note from (
3.6
)–(
3.7
) and Lemma
4.1
that

\displaystyle-\hat{q}+\rho u\hat{\eta}_{\rho}+\rho u^{2}\hat{\eta}_{m}=0\qquad\,\,\mbox{if $|u|\geq k(\rho)$},

(5.78)

\displaystyle\,|-\hat{q}+\rho u\hat{\eta}_{\rho}+\rho u^{2}\hat{\eta}_{m}|\leq C\rho^{\gamma(\rho)+\theta(\rho)}\leq C\big(\rho+\rho^{\gamma_{2}+\theta_{2}}\big)\qquad\,\,\mbox{if $|u|\leq k(\rho)$}.

(5.79)

Then it follows from (
5.78
)–(
5.79
) and Corollary
5.7
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\int_{r}^{b(t)}\big(-\hat{q}+\rho u\hat{\eta}_{\rho}+\rho u^{2}\hat{\eta}_{m}\big)\,y\mathrm{d}y\Big|\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{b(t)}\big(\rho+\rho^{\gamma_{2}+\theta_{2}}\big)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T),

(5.80)

where we have used
\theta_{2}\in(0,1)
since
\gamma_{2}\in(\frac{6}{5},3)
.
Combining (
5.70
)–(
5.2
), (
5.74
)–(
5.2
), and (
5.2
),
we obtain that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\hat{q}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)
,
which, along with (
5.7
) and Lemma
4.1
, gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{[d,D]\cap\{r:|u|\geq k(\rho)\}}\rho|u|^{3}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{[d,D]\cap\{r:|u|\geq k(\rho)\}}\hat{q}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\hat{q}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t-2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{[d,D]\cap\{r:|u|<k(\rho)\}}\hat{q}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}(\rho+\rho^{\gamma_{2}+1})\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T).

(5.81)

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{[d,D]\cap\{r:\,|u|\leq k(\rho)\}}\rho|u|^{3}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\gamma(\rho)+\theta(\rho)}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho+\rho p(\rho)\big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(M,E_{0},T).

(5.82)

Combining (
5.2
) with (
5.2
), we obtain that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho|u|^{3}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)
.
This completes the proof of Lemma
5.8
.
\hfill\square

6
Existence of Global Weak Solutions of CNSPEs

In this section, for fixed
\varepsilon>0
, we take the limit:
b\rightarrow\infty

to obtain the global existence of solutions of the Cauchy problem for (
1.10
).
Meanwhile, some uniform estimates in Theorem
2.1
are obtained.
To take the limit, some careful attention is required,
since the weak solutions may involve the vacuum.
We use similar compactness arguments as in
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
;
Chen-Wang-2020

to handle the limit:
b\rightarrow\infty
.
Throughout this section, we denote the smooth solutions of (
5.1
)–(
5.6
)
as
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},u^{\varepsilon,b})
for simplicity.

First of all, we extend our solutions
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},u^{\varepsilon,b})

to be zero on
([0,T]\times[0,\infty))\backslash\Omega_{T}
.
It follows from Lemma
5.5
that

\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\min_{t\in[0,T]}b(t)=\infty,

(6.1)

which implies that domain
[0,T]\times[a,b(t)]
expands to
[0,T]\times(0,\infty)

as
b\rightarrow\infty
.
That is, for any
set
K\Subset(0,\infty)
, when
b\gg 1,K\Subset(a,b(t))
for all
t\in[0,T]
.
Now we define

(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b},\Phi^{\varepsilon,b})(t,\mathbf{x}):=(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r),m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r},\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r))\qquad\text{for }r=|\mathbf{x}|,

where
m^{\varepsilon,b}:=\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}
.
Then it is direct to check that the corresponding functions

(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b},\Phi^{\varepsilon,b})(t,\mathbf{x})
are classical solutions
of

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}+\nabla\cdot\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b}=0,\\
&\partial_{t}\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b}+\nabla\cdot\Big(\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\Big)+\nabla P(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}=\varepsilon\nabla\cdot\Big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}D\big(\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b}}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\big)\Big),\\
&\Delta\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}=\rho^{\varepsilon,b},\end{aligned}\right.

for
(t,\mathbf{x})\in[0,\infty)\times\Omega_{t}
with
\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon,b}|_{\partial B_{a}(\mathbf{0})}=0
.

Based on the estimates obtained in §
5
,
by the same arguments as in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, §4)
,
we have

Lemma 6.1
.

For fixed
\varepsilon>0
, as
b\to\infty

(
up to a subsequence
)
, there exists a vector function
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})(t,r))
such that

(i)

(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}},\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\to(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}},\rho^{\varepsilon})
a.e. and strongly in

C(0,T;L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p})

for any
p\in[1,\infty)
, where
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p}
denotes
L^{p}(K)

for any compact set
K\Subset(0,\infty)
. In particular,
\rho^{\varepsilon}\geq 0
a.e. on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
.

(ii)

The pressure function sequence
P(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p}(\mathbb{R}))
for all
p\in[1,\infty]
,
and

P(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\longrightarrow P(\rho^{\varepsilon})\quad\text{ strongly in }L^{p}(0,T;L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p}(\mathbb{R}))\qquad\text{for }p\in[1,\infty).

(iii)

The momentum function sequence
m^{\varepsilon,b}

converges strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p}(\mathbb{R}))

to

m^{\varepsilon}
for all
p\in[1,\infty)
.
In particular,

m^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)=(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b})(t,r)\longrightarrow m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\qquad\text{ {\it a.e.} in }[0,T]\times(0,\infty).

(iv)

m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0
a.e. on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}
.
Furthermore, there exists a function
u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
such that

m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
a.e.,

u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0
a.e. on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}
, and

\displaystyle m^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow m^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{ strongly in $L^{2}\left(0,T;L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p}(\mathbb{R})\right)$ for $p\in[1,\infty)$},

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\longrightarrow\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}=\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{ strongly in $L^{2}(0,T;L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}))$}.

Let
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})(t,r)
be the limit function obtained above. Using (
5.13
), (
6.1
),
Lemmas
5.1
–
5.6
,
5.8
, and
6.1
,
Corollaries
5.2
–
5.3
and
5.7
, Fatou’s lemma, and the lower semicontinuity,
we conclude the proof of (
2.22
)–(
2.25
).

Now we show the convergence of the potential functions
\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}
.
Using the similar arguments as in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, Lemma 4.6)
, we have

Lemma 6.2
.

For fixed
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a function
\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})=\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
such that, as
b\to\infty

(
up to a subsequence
)
,

\displaystyle\Phi^{\varepsilon,b}\,{\rightharpoonup}\,\Phi^{\varepsilon}\quad\text{weak-star in $L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))$
and weakly in $L^{2}{(0,T;H_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}$},

(6.2)

\displaystyle\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)r^{2}\,\to\,\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}(t,r)r^{2}=\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\qquad\text{in $C_{\mathrm{loc}}([0,T]\times[0,\infty))$},

(6.3)

\displaystyle\|\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\text{for $t\geq 0$}.

(6.4)

Moreover, since
\gamma_{2}>\frac{6}{5}
,

\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\big(\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}-\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}\big)(t,r)\big|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\rightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as $b\rightarrow\infty$ $($up to a subsequence$)$}.

(6.5)

Using (
6.5
), Fatou’s lemma, and Lemmas
5.1
and
6.1
,
we obtain the following energy inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\Big)(t,r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,r)|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m_{0}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}e(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon})\Big)(r)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\Phi_{0}^{\varepsilon}(r)|^{2}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r.

(6.6)

We denote

(\rho^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon},\Phi^{\varepsilon})(t,\mathbf{x}):=(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r),m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r},\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,r)).

Then (
2.20
) follows directly from (
6.6
).
Moreover, we can prove that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon},\Phi^{\varepsilon})
is a global weak solution of
the Cauchy problem (
1.10
) and (
2.17
)–(
2.18
) in the sense of Definition
2.1
.
In fact, by the same arguments in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, Remark 4.7 and Lemmas 4.9–4.11)
,
we have

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
, and let
\zeta(t,\mathbf{x})\in C_{0}^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3})

be any smooth function with compact support. Then

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{2},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{2},\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{1},\mathbf{x})\zeta(t_{1},\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\rho^{\varepsilon}\zeta_{t}+\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\zeta)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\mathrm{d}t.

(6.7)

Moreover, (
2.21
) holds, and the total mass is conserved
:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=M\qquad\,\text{for $t\geq 0$}.

(6.8)

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
\Psi(t,\mathbf{x})\in(C_{0}^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))^{3}
be any smooth function
with compact support so that
\Psi(T,\mathbf{x})=0
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{4}}\Big\{\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi+\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi+P(\rho^{\varepsilon})\nabla\cdot\Psi-\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Psi\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{M}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\Psi(0,\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}_{+}}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\big(\Delta\Psi+\nabla(\nabla\cdot\Psi)\big)+\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\big(\frac{\mathcal{M}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\Psi\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{4}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big\{V^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big(I_{3\times 3}-\frac{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{x}}{r^{2}}\Big)\Big\}:\nabla\Psi\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\mathrm{d}t

(6.9)

with
V^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
as a function satisfying

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|V^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(E_{0},M),

where
C\left(E_{0},M\right)>0
is a constant independent of
T>0
.

Lemma 6.5
.

It follows from (
6.3
) that
\Phi^{\varepsilon}
satisfies Poisson’s equation in the classical sense
except for the origin
:

\Delta\Phi^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})
for
(t,\mathbf{x})\in[0,\infty)\times(\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\{\mathbf{0}\})
.
Moreover, for any smooth function
\xi(\mathbf{x})\in C_{0}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
with compact support,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\cdot\nabla\xi(\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})\,\xi(\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\qquad\text{for }t\geq 0.

(6.10)

7
\,W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p}
–Compactness of Weak Entropy Dissipation Measures

In this section, using
the estimates of the weak entropy pairs obtained in Lemmas
4.5
,
4.7
, and
4.10
, we establish the compactness
of weak entropy dissipation measures:

\partial_{t}\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
for each weak entropy pair
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
.
Unfortunately, we fail to obtain the same
H_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1}
-compactness as in
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
;
Chen-Wang-2020
, since we only obtain that

q^{\varepsilon}
is uniformly bounded in
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}
from
Lemma
4.10
and Corollary
5.7
.
Instead, using similar arguments as in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, Lemma 4.2)
,
we can obtain the compactness in
W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p}
for any
p\in[1,2)
.

Lemma 7.1

(Compactness of the entropy dissipation measures)
.

Let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be a weak entropy pair defined in
(
4.63
) for any smooth and compactly supported function
\psi(s)
on
\mathbb{R}
. Then, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
,

\partial_{t}\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\qquad\text{is compact in }W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})\quad\mbox{for any $p\in[1,2)$}.

(7.1)

Proof.

To establish (
7.1
), we first need to study the equation:

\partial_{t}\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
in the distributional sense,
which is more complicated than that in
Chen-Perepelitsa-2010
;
Chen-Perepelitsa-2015
.
For simplicity, we denote
(\eta^{\varepsilon,b},q^{\varepsilon,b})=(\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b}),q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b}))

and
(\eta^{\varepsilon},q^{\varepsilon})=(\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon}),q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon}))
.
We divide it into four steps.

1. Considering
\eqref{2.1}_{1}\times\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}+\eqref{2.1}_{2}\times\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})+\partial_{r}q(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})+\frac{2}{r}m^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}+u^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\big)

\displaystyle=-\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}{r^{2}}\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y+\varepsilon\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\Big\{\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{2}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b})\big)_{r}-\frac{2}{r}\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big\},

(7.2)

where
\rho^{\varepsilon,b}
is understood to be zero in domain
[0,T]\times[0,a)
so that

\int_{a}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z
can be written as

\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z
in the potential term.
Let
\phi(t,r)\in C_{0}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\right)
and
b\gg 1

so that
\operatorname{supp}\phi(t,\cdot)\in(a,b(t))
.
Multiplying (
7.2
) by
\phi
and integrating by parts yield

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}(\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon,b}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon,b})\phi\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\frac{2}{r}m^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}+u^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b})\phi\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}\big(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{2}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\,-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\big(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{2}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\frac{2}{r}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\phi\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\,-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}{r^{2}}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\Big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)\phi\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t:=\sum_{i=1}^{5}I_{i}^{\varepsilon,b}.

(7.3)

2. From Lemmas
4.2
and
6.1
,
it is clear to see that

\eta^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow\eta^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{{\it a.e.} in }\{(t,r)\,:\rho^{\varepsilon}\neq 0\}\quad\text{as }b\to\infty.

(7.4)

In
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}
, it follows from Lemmas
4.5
and
4.7
that

|\eta^{\varepsilon,b}|\leq C\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow 0=\eta^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{as $b\to\infty$}.

(7.5)

Combining (
7.4
)–(
7.5
), we obtain

\eta^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow\eta^{\varepsilon}\qquad\,\text{{\it a.e.} as $b\to\infty$}.

(7.6)

Similarly, it follows from Lemmas
4.3
,
4.10
, and
6.1

that

q^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow q^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{{\it a.e.} as $b\to\infty$}.

(7.7)

For
\gamma_{2}\in(1,3)
and any subset

K\Subset(0,\infty)
, it follows from Lemmas
4.5
,

4.7
,
4.10
, and Corollary
5.7
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\big(|\eta^{\varepsilon,b}|^{\gamma_{2}+1}+|q^{\varepsilon,b}|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(K)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\big(1+|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|^{\gamma_{2}+1}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T),

which implies that

(\eta^{\varepsilon,b},q^{\varepsilon,b})

is uniformly bounded in
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
.
This, with (
7.6
)–(
7.7
), yields that, up to a subsequence,

(\eta^{\varepsilon,b},q^{\varepsilon,b})\rightharpoonup(\eta^{\varepsilon},q^{\varepsilon})\qquad\text{in $L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$ as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

Thus, for any
\phi\in C_{0}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
,
as
b\rightarrow\infty
(up to a subsequence),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\big(\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon,b}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\big(\eta^{\varepsilon,b}\partial_{t}\phi+q^{\varepsilon,b}\partial_{r}\phi\big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\longrightarrow-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\big(\eta^{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}\phi+q^{\varepsilon}\partial_{r}\phi\big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t.

(7.8)

Furthermore,
(\eta^{\varepsilon},q^{\varepsilon})
is uniformly bounded in
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
, which implies that

\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}\quad\text{ is uniformly bounded in }\varepsilon>0\text{ in }W_{\text{loc }}^{-1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}).

(7.9)

Since
q^{\varepsilon,b}
is only uniformly bounded in
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})

in view of Lemma
4.10
and Corollary
5.7
, we cannot conclude that

\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}
is uniformly bounded in
\varepsilon>0
in
W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
with
p>2
,
which is different from
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
.

3. For the terms on the RHS of (
7.3
), using Lemmas
4.5
,
4.7
, and
4.10
,
and similar calculations as in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, Lemma 5.11)
, we obtain that

\displaystyle I_{1}^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\frac{2}{r}m^{\varepsilon}(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon})\phi\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\qquad\text{up to a subsequence as $b\rightarrow\infty$},

(7.10)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\frac{2}{r}\big|m^{\varepsilon}(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon})\big|^{\frac{7}{6}}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T),

and there exist local bounded Radon measures
(\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon})

on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
such that, as
b\rightarrow\infty
(up to a subsequence),

\displaystyle-(\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{2}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}),\,\frac{2\varepsilon}{r}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b},\,\kappa\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}{r^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,z)\,z^{2}\mathrm{d}z)\rightharpoonup(\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon}).

In addition, for
i=1,2,3
,

\mu_{i}^{\varepsilon}((0,T)\times V)\leq C_{\psi}(K,T,E_{0})\qquad\,\,\mbox{for any open subset $V\subset K$}.

(7.11)

Then, up to a subsequence, we have

I_{2}^{\varepsilon,b}+I_{4}^{\varepsilon,b}+I_{5}^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow\langle\,\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon},\,\phi\rangle\qquad\text{ as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

(7.12)

Moreover, there exists a function
f^{\varepsilon}
such that, as
b\rightarrow\infty
(up to a subsequence),

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\varepsilon}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\big(u_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}+\frac{2}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\rightharpoonup f^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{ weakly in $L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\frac{4}{3}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$},

(7.13)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\left|f^{\varepsilon}\right|^{\frac{4}{3}}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C_{\psi}(K,M,E_{0},T).

Then it follows from (
7.13
) that

I_{3}^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}f^{\varepsilon}\phi_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\qquad\text{ as $b\rightarrow\infty$ (up to a subsequence)}.

(7.14)

4. Taking
b\rightarrow\infty
(up to a subsequence) on both sides of (
7.3
),
it follows from (
7
), (
7.10
), (
7.12
), and (
7.14
) that

\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}=-\frac{2}{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon})+\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}f_{r}^{\varepsilon}

in the distributional sense. It follows from (
7.10
)–(
7.11
) that

-\frac{2}{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}\left(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon}\right)+\mu_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{3}^{\varepsilon}

(7.15)

is a locally uniformly bounded Radon measure sequence.
From (
7.13
), we know that

\sqrt{\varepsilon}f_{r}^{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ in $W_{\operatorname{loc}}^{-1,\frac{4}{3}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$ as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0$}.

(7.16)

Then it follows from (
7.15
)–(
7.16
) that the sequence:

\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{ is confined in a compact subset of $W_{\text{loc }}^{-1,p_{2}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$
for some $p_{2}\in(1,2)$},

(7.17)

which also implies that

\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{is compact in
$W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$ with $1\leq p\leq p_{2}$}.

(7.18)

On the other hand, the interpolation compactness theorem (
cf.

Chen-1986
;
Ding-Chen-Luo
) indicates that,
for
p_{2}>1,p_{1}\in\left(p_{2},\infty\right]
, and
p_{0}\in\left[p_{2},p_{1}\right)
,

\displaystyle\big(\text{compact set of }W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p_{2}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})\big)\cap\big(\text{bounded set of }W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})\big)

\displaystyle\quad\subset\big(\text{compact set of }W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})\big),

which is a generalization of Murat’s lemma in
Murat-1978
;
Tartar-1979
.
Combining this theorem for
1<p_{2}<2
and
p_{1}=2
with the facts in (
7.9
) and (
7.17
), we conclude that

\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{is compact in $W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$ with $p_{2}\leq p<2$}.

(7.19)

Combining (
7.19
) with (
7.18
), we conclude (
7.1
).

\hfill\square

8

L^{p}
Compensated Compactness Framework

In this section, with the help of our understanding of the singularities of the entropy kernel
and entropy flux kernel obtained in Lemma
4.14
, we now establish the

L^{p}
compensated compactness framework and
complete the proof of Theorem
2.2
.
The key ingredient is to prove the reduction of the Young measure.
The arguments are similar to

(
Schrecker-Schulz-2019
, §4)
and
(
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
, §7)
,
based on
Chen-Perepelitsa-2010
;
Chen-LeFloch-2000
,
so we only sketch the proof for self-containedness.

We denote the upper half-plane by

\mathbb{H}:=\{(\rho,u)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,:\,\rho>0\}

and consider the following subset of continuous functions:

\overline{C}(\mathbb{H}):=\left\{\phi\in C(\overline{\mathbb{H}})\,:\,\,\begin{array}[]{l}\phi(\rho,u)\,\text{ is constant on the vacuum states }\,\{\rho=0\}\,\text{ and}\\
\text{the map: }(\rho,u)\,\mapsto\,\lim\limits_{s\rightarrow\infty}\phi(s\rho,su)\text{ belongs to }C(\mathbb{S}^{1}\cap\overline{\mathbb{H}})\end{array}\right\},

where
\mathbb{S}^{1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is the unit circle.
Since
\overline{C}(\mathbb{H})
is a complete sub-ring of the continuous functions on
\mathbb{H}
containing the constant functions,
there exists a compactification
\overline{\mathcal{H}}
of
\mathbb{H}
such that
C(\overline{\mathcal{H}})
is isometrically
isomorphic to
\overline{C}(\mathbb{H})
(
cf
.
LeFloch-Westdickenberg-2007
),
written
C(\overline{\mathcal{H}})\cong\overline{C}(\mathbb{H})
.
The topology of
\overline{\mathcal{H}}
is the weak-star topology induced by
C(\overline{\mathcal{H}})
,
i.e.
,
a sequence
\{v_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\overline{\mathcal{H}}
converges to
v\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}

if
|\varphi(v_{n})-\varphi(v)|\rightarrow 0
for all
\varphi\in C(\overline{\mathcal{H}})
, which
is separable and metrizable (
cf
.
LeFloch-Westdickenberg-2007
).
Denote by
V
the weak-star closure of the vacuum states
\{(\rho,u)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,:\,\rho=0\}
and
define
\mathcal{H}:=\mathbb{H}\cup V
. In view of the functions that lie in
\overline{C}(\mathbb{H})
,
the topology of
\overline{\mathcal{H}}
does not distinguish points in

V
.
Since
\overline{\mathcal{H}}
is homeomorphic to a compact metric space,
we may apply the fundamental theorem of Young measures
in Alberti-Müller
(
Alberti-Muller-2001
, Theorem 2.4)
.

Lemma 8.1

(
(
Alberti-Muller-2001
, Theorem 2.4)
)
.

Given any sequence of measurable functions
\left(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}\right):\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{H}}
,
there exists a subsequence
(
still denoted
)

(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})

generating a Young measure
\nu_{(t,r)}\in\operatorname{Prob}(\overline{\mathcal{H}})
in the sense that,
for any
\phi\in\overline{C}(\mathbb{H})
,

\phi(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r),u^{\varepsilon}(t,r))\,\overset{*}{\rightharpoonup}\,\int_{\overline{\mathcal{H}}}\iota(\phi)(\rho,u)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\rho,u)\qquad\text{ in $L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$},

where
\iota:\overline{C}(\mathbb{H})\to C(\overline{\mathcal{H}})
is an isometrically isomorphism.
Moreover, sequence
(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})
converges to
(\rho,\rho u):\,\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\to\overline{\mathcal{H}}

a.e. if and only if

\nu_{(t,r)}(\rho,u)=\delta_{(\rho(t,r),m(t,r))}\qquad\text{a.e. $(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}$},

in the phase coordinates
(\rho,m)
with
m=\rho u
.

From now on, we often use the same letter
\nu_{(t,r)}
for an element of

\big(\overline{C}(\mathbb{H})\big)^{*}
or
\big(C(\overline{\mathcal{H}})\big)^{*}
,
and use the same letter for
\iota(\phi)
and
\phi
for simplicity,
when no confusion arises.

The following lemma shows that the Young measure
\nu_{(t,r)}
, generated by the sequence of
measurable approximate solutions
(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})
satisfying the assumptions of Theorem
2.2
,
is only supported on the interior of
\mathcal{H}
.
Moreover, the Young measure
\nu_{(t,r)}
can be extended to a larger class of test functions
than just
\overline{C}(\mathbb{H})
. This is proved in
(
Chen-Perepelitsa-2010
, Proposition 5.1)
;
also see

(
LeFloch-Westdickenberg-2007
, Proposition 2.3)
.

Lemma 8.2

(
(
Chen-Perepelitsa-2010
, Proposition 5.1)
)
.

The following statements hold
:

(i)

For the Young measure
\nu_{(t,r)}
generated by the sequence of measurable approximate
solutions
(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})
satisfying the assumptions in
Theorem
2.2
,

(t,r)\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{H}}(\rho^{\gamma_{2}+1}+\rho|u|^{3})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\rho,u)\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}).

(ii)

Let
\phi(\rho,u)
be a function such that

(a)

\phi
is continuous on
\overline{\mathbb{H}}
and
\phi=0
on
\partial\mathbb{H}
,

(b)

there exists a constant
\mathfrak{a}>0
such that
\operatorname{supp}\phi\subset\{u+k(\rho)\geq-\mathfrak{a},u-k(\rho)\leq\mathfrak{a}\}
,

(c)

|\phi(\rho,u)|\leq\rho^{\beta(\gamma_{2}+1)}
for all
(\rho,u)
with large
\rho
and some
\beta\in(0,1)
.

Then
\phi
is
\nu_{(t,r)}
–integrable for
(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
a.e. and

\phi(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r),u^{\varepsilon}(t,r))\rightharpoonup\int_{\mathbb{H}}\phi(\rho,u)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\rho,u)\qquad\text{in }\;L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}).

(iii)

\nu_{(t,r)}\in\operatorname{Prob}(\mathcal{H})
for
(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
a.e., that is,

\,\nu_{(t,r)}\big(\overline{\mathcal{H}}\backslash(\mathbb{H}\cup V)\big)=0.

We now prove the commutation relation. Since we only have the
W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,p}
–compactness
of the entropy dissipation measures for
p\in[1,2)
,
the classical div-curl lemma in
Murat-1978
fails to obtain
the commutation relation.
Thus, we adopt an improved version of the div-curl lemma.

Lemma 8.3

(
(
Conti-Dolzmann-Muller-2011
, Theorem)
)
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open bounded set, and
p,q\in(1,\infty)
with
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1
.
Let
\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}
are sequences of vector fields such that

\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\mathbf{v}\,\,\text{ in }L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\mathbf{w}\,\,\text{ in }L^{q}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})\qquad\text{ as }\varepsilon\to 0.

Suppose that
\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}
is equi-integrable uniformly in
\varepsilon
, and

\begin{split}&\operatorname{div}\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\quad\,\,\,\text{ is $($pre-$)$compact in }W^{-1,1}(\Omega),\\
&\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\quad\text{ is $($pre-$)$compact in }W^{-1,1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}).\end{split}

Then

\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\,\rightharpoonup\,\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{w}

in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
.

Lemma 8.4

(Commutation relation)
.

Let
\{(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})\}_{\varepsilon>0}
be the measurable approximate solutions
satisfying the assumptions of
Theorem
2.2
,
and let
\nu_{(t,r)}
be a Young measure generated by
the family
\{(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})\}_{\varepsilon>0}
in
Lemma
8.2
. Then

\overline{\chi(s_{1})\sigma(s_{2})-\chi(s_{2})\sigma(s_{1})}=\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\;\overline{\sigma(s_{2})}-\overline{\chi(s_{2})}\;\overline{\sigma(s_{1})}

(8.1)

for all
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, where

\displaystyle\overline{f}:=\int f\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}
,

\chi(s_{i})=\chi(\cdot,\cdot-s_{i})
, and
\sigma(s_{i})=\sigma(\cdot,\cdot-s_{i})
.

Proof.

For any
\psi\in C_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, it follows from Lemmas
4.5
,
4.7
, and
4.10
that

|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho,\qquad|q^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\big(\rho+\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\big).

(8.2)

It is clear that the support of
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
is contained
in
\left\{k(\rho)+u\geq-L,u-k(\rho)\leq L\right\}
for some
L>0

depending only on
\operatorname{supp}\,\psi
.
For any
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in C_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we consider the sequences of vector fields:

\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}=(\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}),q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})),\qquad\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}=(q^{\psi_{2}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}),-\eta^{\psi_{2}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})).

Noting
\rho^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1+\gamma_{2}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
and (
8.2
),
we see that both
\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}
are uniformly bounded
sequences in
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
.
Moreover, by Lemma
8.2
and the uniqueness of weak limits, we obtain

\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup(\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}},\overline{q^{\psi_{1}}})\,\,\,\text{ in $L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$},\qquad\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup(\overline{q^{\psi_{2}}},-\overline{\eta^{\psi_{2}}})\,\,\,\text{ in $L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$}.

By direct calculation, we see that

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}=\partial_{t}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})\quad\,\,\,\text{are compact in }W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}=\partial_{t}\eta^{\psi_{2}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q^{\psi_{2}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})\quad\text{are compact in }W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{-1,1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}).

Using (
8.2
), we obtain that

|\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}|\leq C\big((\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2+\theta_{2}}\big)

for
\rho>0

which, with (
2.25
), yields that
\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\frac{1+\gamma_{2}}{2+\theta_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
uniformly in
\varepsilon
.
Thus,
\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}
is equi-integrable uniformly in
\varepsilon
since
\frac{1+\gamma_{2}}{2+\theta_{2}}>1
. It follows from Lemma
8.3
that

\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\,\rightarrow\,\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}}\;\overline{q^{\psi_{2}}}-\overline{\eta^{\psi_{2}}}\;\overline{q^{\psi_{1}}}\qquad\text{in the sense of distributions in
$\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}$}.

(8.3)

On the other hand, using (
8.2
) and Lemma
8.2
, we find that

\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\,\rightarrow\,\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}q^{\psi_{2}}-\eta^{\psi_{2}}q^{\psi_{1}}}\qquad\text{ in $L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$},

which, with (
8.3
), yields that

\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}q^{\psi_{2}}-\eta^{\psi_{2}}q^{\psi_{1}}}=\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}}\;\overline{q^{\psi_{2}}}-\overline{\eta^{\psi_{2}}}\;\overline{q^{\psi_{1}}}.

(8.4)

It follows from (
8.4
) and Fubini’s theorem that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Big(\overline{\chi(s_{1})\sigma(s_{2})-\chi(s_{2})\sigma(s_{1})}-\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\;\overline{\sigma(s_{2})}+\overline{\chi(s_{2})}\;\overline{\sigma(s_{1})}\Big)\psi_{1}(s_{1})\psi_{2}(s_{2})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}s_{2}=0.

Since
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in C_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
are arbitrary, we conclude

\overline{\chi(s_{1})\sigma(s_{2})-\chi(s_{2})\sigma(s_{1})}=\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\;\overline{\sigma(s_{2})}-\overline{\chi(s_{2})}\;\overline{\sigma(s_{1})}\qquad\mbox{for any $s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}$}.

This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

Theorem 8.5

(Reduction of the Young measure)
.

Let
\nu_{(t,r)}\in\mathrm{Prob}(\mathcal{H})
be the Young
measure generated
by sequence
\{(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})\}_{\varepsilon>0}
in
Lemma
8.2
.
Then either
\nu_{(t,r)}
is contained in
V
or the support of
\nu_{(t,r)}
is a single point in
\mathbb{H}
.

Proof.
The proof is similar
to
Chen-LeFloch-2000
;
Lions-Perthame-Souganidis-1996
;
Schrecker-Schulz-2019
;
Schrecker-Schulz-2020
.
Since the estimates of the entropy kernel and entropy flux kernel
are different, we sketch the proof for self-containedness.

Taking
s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
and multiplying (
8.1
) by
\overline{\chi(s_{3})}
, one obtains

\overline{\chi(s_{3})}\;\overline{\chi(s_{1})\,\sigma(s_{2})-\chi(s_{2})\,\sigma(s_{1})}=\overline{\chi(s_{3})}\;\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\;\overline{\sigma(s_{2})}-\overline{\chi(s_{3})}\;\overline{\chi(s_{2})}\;\overline{\sigma(s_{1})}.

Cyclically permuting index
s_{j}
and adding the resultant equations together, we have

\displaystyle\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\;\overline{\chi(s_{2})\sigma(s_{3})-\chi(s_{3})\sigma(s_{2})}

\displaystyle=\overline{\chi(s_{3})}\;\overline{\chi(s_{2})\sigma(s_{1})-\chi(s_{1})\sigma(s_{2})}-\overline{\chi(s_{2})}\;\overline{\chi(s_{3})\sigma(s_{1})-\chi(s_{1})\sigma(s_{3})}.

Applying the fractional derivative operators
P_{2}:=\partial_{s_{2}}^{\lambda_{1}+1}

and
P_{3}:=\partial_{s_{3}}^{\lambda_{1}+1}
in the sense of distributions to obtain

\displaystyle\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\;\overline{P_{2}\chi(s_{2})\,P_{3}\sigma(s_{3})-P_{3}\chi(s_{3})\,P_{2}\sigma(s_{2})}

\displaystyle=\overline{P_{3}\chi(s_{3})}\;\overline{P_{2}\chi(s_{2})\,\sigma(s_{1})-\chi(s_{1})\,P_{2}\sigma(s_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad-\overline{P_{2}\chi(s_{2})}\;\overline{P_{3}\chi(s_{3})\,\sigma(s_{1})-\chi(s_{1})\,P_{3}\sigma(s_{3})},

(8.5)

where, for example, distribution
\overline{P_{2}\chi(s_{2})}
is defined by

\langle\overline{P_{2}\chi(s_{2})},\psi\rangle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\partial_{s_{2}}^{\lambda_{1}}\chi(s_{2})}\,\psi^{\prime}(s_{2})\,\mathrm{d}s_{2}\qquad\,\text{for $\psi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R})$}.

We take two standard but different functions
\phi_{2},\phi_{3}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(-1,1)
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{j}(s_{j})\,\mathrm{d}s_{j}=1

with
\phi_{j}\geq 0
for
j=2,3
.
For
\tau>0
, denote
\phi_{j}^{\tau}(s_{j}):=\frac{1}{\tau}\phi_{j}(\frac{s_{j}}{\tau})
.
As indicated in
LeFloch-Westdickenberg-2007
, we can always choose
\phi_{2}
and
\phi_{3}
such that

Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{s_{2}}\big(\phi_{2}(s_{2})\phi_{3}(s_{3})-\phi_{2}(s_{3})\phi_{3}(s_{2})\big)\,\mathrm{d}s_{3}\mathrm{d}s_{2}>0.

(8.6)

Multiplying (
8
) by
\phi_{2}^{\tau}(s_{1}-s_{2})\phi_{3}^{\tau}(s_{1}-s_{3})

and integrating the resultant equation with respect to
(s_{2},s_{3})
yield

\displaystyle\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\;\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau}}

\displaystyle=\overline{P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}}\;\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}\,\sigma_{1}-\chi_{1}\,P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau}}-\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}}\;\overline{P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,\sigma_{1}-\chi_{1}\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}},

(8.7)

where we have used the notion:

\overline{P_{j}\chi_{j}^{\tau}}=\overline{P_{j}\chi_{j}}*\phi_{j}^{\tau}(s_{1})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\partial_{s_{j}}^{\lambda}\chi(s_{j})}\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(\frac{s_{1}-s_{j}}{\tau})\,\mathrm{d}s_{j}

for
j=2,3
.

Multiplying (
8
) by
\psi(s_{1})\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R})
,
integrating the resultant equation with respect to
s_{1}
,
then taking limit
\tau\to 0
and applying Lemmas
8.8
–
8.9
below,
we obtain

Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\int_{\mathcal{H}}Z(\rho)\sum_{\pm}(K^{\pm})^{2}\,\overline{\chi(u\pm k(\rho))}\,\psi(u\pm k(\rho))\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\rho,u)=0.

(8.8)

Noting that
Z(\rho)>0
for
\rho>0
from Lemma
8.7
below,

Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})>0
from (
8.6
), and
\psi(s)
is an arbitrary test function,
we deduce from (
8.8
) that

\int_{\mathcal{H}}Z(\rho)\,\overline{\chi(u\pm k(\rho))}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\rho,u)=0.

(8.9)

We define
\mathbb{S}=\{s\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\overline{\chi(s)}>0\}
.
It follows from
Schrecker-Schulz-2019
that
\mathbb{S}
admits the representation:

\mathbb{S}=\left\{s\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,u-k(\rho)<s<u+k(\rho)\,\,\text{ with $(\rho,u)\in\operatorname{supp}\nu_{(t,r)}$}\right\}.

For the case:
\mathbb{S}=\emptyset
, it is clear that
\overline{\chi(s)}=0
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}

so that
\operatorname{supp}\nu_{(t,r)}\subset V
,
since
\chi(s)>0
for all
\rho>0
and
s\in(u-k(\rho),u+k(\rho))
.

For the case:
\mathbb{S}\neq\emptyset
, it follows from (
8.15
) below
that
s\mapsto\overline{\chi(s)}
is a continuous map. Then
\mathbb{S}
is an open set
so that
\mathbb{S}
is at most a countable union of open intervals. Thus, we may write

\mathbb{S}=\underset{k}{\mathbin{\scalebox{1.5}{$\cup$}}}(\zeta_{k},\,\xi_{k})

for at most countably many numbers
\zeta_{k}:=u_{k}-k(\rho_{k})
and

\xi_{k}:=u_{k}+k(\rho_{k})
with
(\rho_{k},u_{k})\in\mathrm{supp}\nu_{(t,r)}

in the extended real line
\mathbb{R}\cup\{\pm\infty\}

such that
\zeta_{k}<\xi_{k}\leq\zeta_{k+1}
for all
k
.
For later use, we denote the Riemann invariants
z(\rho,u):=u-k(\rho)
and
w(\rho,u):=u+k(\rho)
.
Thus, noting that
\mathrm{supp}\,\chi(s)=\{(\rho,u)\,:\,z(\rho,u)\leq s\leq w(\rho,u)\}
,
we obtain

\operatorname{supp}\nu_{(t,r)}\subset\underset{k}{\mathbin{\scalebox{1.5}{$\cup$}}}\{(\rho,u)\in\mathbb{H}\,:\,\zeta_{k}\leq z(\rho,u)<w(\rho,u)\leq\xi_{k}\}\cup V.

If
\zeta_{k}
and
\xi_{k}
are both finite,
due to the fact that
k(\rho)
is a strictly monotone increasing and unbounded function of
\rho
,
it is clear that

\left\{(\rho,u)\,:\,\zeta_{k}\leq z(\rho,u)\leq w(\rho,u)\leq\xi_{k}\right\}
is bounded.
Now we deduce from (
8.9
) that

\operatorname{supp}\nu_{(t,r)}\cap\{(\rho,u)\in\mathbb{H}\,:\,\zeta_{k}<z(\rho,u)<w(\rho,u)<\xi_{k}\}=\emptyset\qquad\text{for all }k.

Thus, the support of measure
\nu_{(t,r)}
must be contained in the vacuum set
V

and at most a countable union of points
P_{k}(\rho_{k},\,u_{k})
:

\operatorname{supp}\nu_{(t,r)}\subset V\cup\big(\underset{\{k:\zeta_{k},\xi_{k}\text{ are finite}\}}{\mathbin{\scalebox{1.5}{$\cup$}}}P_{k}(\rho_{k},u_{k})\big).

Therefore, we may write

\nu_{(t,r)}=\nu_{V}+\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}\delta_{P_{k}}\qquad\,\mbox{for all $\alpha_{k}\in[0,1]$}

(8.10)

with measure
\nu_{V}
supported on the vacuum set
V
.
For later use, we denote

\chi(P_{k},s):=\chi(\rho_{k},u_{k},s),\quad\sigma(P_{k},s):=\sigma(\rho_{k},u_{k},s)\qquad\,\,\text{for $s\in\mathbb{R}$}.

We claim that, if
\chi(P_{k},s)>0
, then
\chi(P_{k^{\prime}},s)=0
for all
k\neq k^{\prime}
.
Indeed, recall that
\operatorname{supp}\chi(s)=\{(\rho,u)\,:\,z(\rho,u)\leq s\leq w(\rho,u)\}

and that
\chi(\rho,u,s)>0
if and only if
z(\rho,u)<s<w(\rho,u)
.
If
\chi(P_{k},s)>0
, then
\zeta_{k}<s<\xi_{k}
.
If, in addition,
\chi(P_{k^{\prime}},s)>0
for some
k\neq k^{\prime}
,
it must hold that
\zeta_{k^{\prime}}<s<\xi_{k^{\prime}}
.
However, since
\xi_{k-1}\leq\zeta_{k}<\xi_{k}\leq\zeta_{k+1}
,
this is impossible for any
P_{k^{\prime}}
with
k^{\prime}\neq k
.

Thus, taking
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\chi(P_{k},s_{1})\chi(P_{k},s_{2})>0
,
we deduce from the commutation relation
(
8.1
) and (
8.10
) that

(\alpha_{k}-\alpha_{k}^{2})\big(\chi(P_{k},s_{1})\sigma(P_{k},s_{2})-\chi(P_{k},s_{2})\sigma(P_{k},s_{1})\big)=0.

Now, choosing
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
such that the second factor in this expression is non-zero,
we obtain that
\alpha_{k}=0
or
1
for all
k
. This completes the proof.
\hfill\square

Combining Theorem
8.5
with Lemma
8.1
,
we conclude that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
converges to
(\rho,m)
almost everywhere.
Moreover, noting that

|m^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}\leq C\big((\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{2}+1}+\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{3}\big)

for any
T,d,D>0
, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}|m^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{2}+1}+\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{3}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(d,D,T),

which implies that
m^{\varepsilon}
is uniformly bounded in
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})

with respect to
\varepsilon
. This implies that (
2.36
) holds.
Therefore, the proof of Theorem
2.2
is complete.

Now, we are going to prove the auxiliary lemmas, Lemmas
8.8
–
8.9
, which
are used in the proof of Theorem
8.5
.
We first recall two useful lemmas in
Chen-LeFloch-2000
;
LeFloch-Westdickenberg-2007
.

Lemma 8.6

(
(
LeFloch-Westdickenberg-2007
, Lemmas 3.8–3.9)
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{R}\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
be a Hölder continuous function
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and let
g\in C_{0}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
be a Hölder continuous function
with compact support. Assume
L_{0}>2
such that
\operatorname{supp}g\subset B_{L_{0}-2}(0)
.

(i)

For
any pair of distributions
T_{2},T_{3}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})

from the following collection
:

(T_{2},T_{3})=(\delta,Q_{3}),\,\,(PV,Q_{3}),\,\,(Q_{2},Q_{3})

with
Q_{2},Q_{3}\in\{H,Ci,\mathfrak{R}\}
,
there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
(\rho,u)
such that

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\tau\in(0,1)}\Big|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g(s_{1})\Big\{\big(

\displaystyle T_{2}(s_{2}-u\pm k(\rho))T_{3}(s_{3}-u\pm k(\rho))

\displaystyle-T_{2}(s_{3}-u\pm k(\rho))T_{3}(s_{2}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{2}^{\tau}*\phi_{3}^{\tau}\Big\}\left(s_{1}\right)\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\|g\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}\big(1+
