\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{R}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\overline{B_{L_{0}}(0)})}\big)^{2}.

(ii)

For
any pair of distributions from

(T_{2},T_{3})=(\delta,\delta),\,\,(PV,PV),\,\,(Q_{2},Q_{3}),\,\,(\delta,PV),\,\,(PV,Q_{3}),

with
Q_{2},Q_{3}\in\{H,Ci,\mathfrak{R}\}
,
there exists
C>0
independent of
(\rho,u)
such that

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\tau\in(0,1)}\Big|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big\{\big((s_{2}-s_{3})T_{2}(s_{2}-u\pm k(\rho))T_{3}(s_{3}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{2}^{\tau}*\phi_{3}^{\tau}\Big\}(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|g\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{R}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\overline{B_{L_{0}}(0)})}\big)^{2}.

Motivated by
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, it follows from Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
, (
1.4
),
and a direct calculation that

\displaystyle D(\rho):=

\displaystyle\,a_{1}(\rho)b_{1}(\rho)-2k(\rho)^{2}(a_{1}(\rho)b_{2}(\rho)-a_{2}(\rho)b_{1}(\rho))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{M_{\lambda_{1}}^{2}}{2(\lambda_{1}+1)}k(\rho)^{-2\lambda_{1}}k^{\prime}(\rho)^{-2}\big(k^{\prime}(\rho)+(\rho k^{\prime}(\rho))^{\prime}\big)>0\qquad\,\text{for $\rho>0$}.

(8.11)

Lemma 8.7

(
(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, Lemmas 4.2–4.3)
)
.

The mollified fractional derivatives of the entropy kernel and
the entropy flux kernel satisfy the following convergence properties
:

(i)

When
0\leq\rho<\infty
,

P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau}\longrightarrow Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})Z(\rho)\sum_{\pm}(K^{\pm})^{2}\delta_{s_{1}=u\pm k(\rho)}

as
\tau\rightarrow 0
weakly-star in measures in
s_{1}
and locally uniformly in
(\rho,u)
,
where
Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})
satisfies (
8.6
),
Z(\rho):=(\lambda_{1}+1)M_{\lambda}^{-2}k(\rho)^{2\lambda}D(\rho)>0

with
D(\rho)
defined in (
8
), and
K^{\pm}\neq 0
are some constants.

(ii)

For
j=2,3
,

\chi_{1}\,P_{j}\sigma_{j}^{\tau}-\sigma_{1}\,P_{j}\chi_{j}^{\tau}
are Hölder continuous
in
(\rho,u,s_{1})
, uniformly in
\tau
,
and there exists a Hölder continuous function
X=X(\rho,u,s_{1})
, independent of the mollifying sequence
\phi_{j}
, such that

\chi(s_{1})P_{j}\sigma_{j}^{\tau}-P_{j}\chi_{j}^{\tau}\sigma(s_{1})\longrightarrow X(\rho,u,s_{1})\qquad\text{as $\tau\rightarrow 0$}

uniformly in
\left(\rho,u,s_{1}\right)
on the sets on which
\rho
is bounded.

Lemma 8.8
.

For any test function
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\tau\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\;\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau}}(s_{1})\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle=Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\int_{\mathcal{H}}Z(\rho)\sum\limits_{\pm}(K^{\pm})^{2}\,\overline{\chi(u\pm k(\rho))}\,\psi(u\pm k(\rho))\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\rho,u),

(8.12)

where
Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})
is defined by (
8.6
) and
Z(\rho)
is given in
Lemma
8.7
.

Proof.
It follows from Lemma
8.7
that,
when
\rho
is bounded,

P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau}\rightarrow Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})Z(\rho)\sum_{\pm}\left(K^{\pm}\right)^{2}\delta_{s_{1}=u\pm k(\rho)}\qquad\text{as }\tau\to 0

locally uniform in
(\rho,u)
and hence pointwise for all
(\rho,u)
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\rightarrow 0}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\langle\,\nu_{(t,r)},\,(P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau}){\mathbf{I}}_{\{\rho\leq\rho^{*}\}}\rangle\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\tau\rightarrow 0}\langle\,\nu_{(t,r)},\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\overline{\chi(s_{1})}(P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau})\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}{\mathbf{I}}_{\{\rho\leq\rho^{*}\}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\,\nu_{(t,r)},\,Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})Z(\rho)\sum_{\pm}(K^{\pm})^{2}\overline{\chi(u\pm k(\rho))}\psi(u\pm k(\rho)){\mathbf{I}}_{\{\rho\leq\rho^{*}\}}\rangle

\displaystyle=Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\sum_{\pm}(K^{\pm})^{2}\langle\,\nu_{(t,r)},\,Z(\rho)\overline{\chi(u\pm k(\rho))}\psi(u\pm k(\rho)){\mathbf{I}}_{\{\rho\leq\rho^{*}\}}\rangle.

(8.13)

For
\rho\geq\rho^{*}
,
we notice that

P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau}=P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}\,P_{3}(\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-u\chi_{3}^{\tau})-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,P_{2}(\sigma_{2}^{\tau}-u\chi_{2}^{\tau}).

(8.14)

Using Lemma
4.14
, we see that (
8.14
) consists of a sum of terms of the form:

A_{i,\pm}(\rho)B_{j,\pm}(\rho)(s_{3}-s_{2})T_{2}(s_{2}-u\pm k(\rho))T_{3}(s_{3}-u\pm k(\rho))

with
T_{2},T_{3}\in\{\delta,\mathrm{PV},H,\mathrm{Ci}\}
, the terms of the form:

A_{i,\pm}(\rho)B_{j,\pm}(\rho)\big(T_{2}(s_{2}-u\pm k(\rho))T_{3}(s_{3}-u\pm k(\rho))-T_{2}(s_{3}-u\pm k(\rho))T_{3}(s_{2}-u\pm k(\rho))\big),

and the terms of the form:

A_{i,\pm}(\rho)B_{j,\pm}(\rho)(s_{3}-u)\big(T_{2}(s_{2}-u\pm k(\rho))T_{3}(s_{3}-u\pm k(\rho))-T_{2}(s_{3}-u\pm k(\rho))T_{3}(s_{2}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)

with
T_{2}\in\{\delta,H,\mathrm{PV},\mathrm{Ci},r_{\chi}\}
and
T_{3}\in\{H,\mathrm{Ci},r_{\sigma}\}
.
We emphasize that, in the last two cases, when
T_{2},T_{3}\in\{r_{\chi},r_{\sigma}\}
,

A_{i,\pm}(\rho)B_{j,\pm}(\rho)
or
A_{i,\pm}(\rho)B_{j,\pm}(\rho)(s_{k}-u)
should be
replaced by
1
.

Before applying Lemma
8.6
, we now show that
\overline{\chi(s)}
is Hölder continuous.
In fact, it follows from Corollary
4.12
and Lemma
8.2
that,
for any
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\alpha\in(0,\min\{\lambda_{1},1\}]
,

\sup_{s,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{|\overline{\chi(s)}-\overline{\chi(s^{\prime})}|}{|s-s^{\prime}|^{\alpha}}=\int_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{|\chi(s)-\chi(s^{\prime})|}{|s-s^{\prime}|^{\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}\leq\int_{\mathcal{H}}C\big(1+\rho|\ln\rho|\big)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}<\infty,

(8.15)

which implies that
\overline{\chi(s)}
is Hölder continuous.
Hence, using Lemma
8.6
and the fact that
|s_{j}-u|\leq k(\rho)
for
j=2,3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\overline{\chi(s_{1})}(P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau})\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}{\mathbf{I}}_{\{\rho\geq\rho^{*}\}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\max_{j,k,\pm}\Big\{|A_{j,\pm}k(\rho)|\big(|B_{k,\pm}|+\|r_{\sigma}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{C^{\alpha_{1}}(\overline{B_{L_{0}}})}\big),

\displaystyle\|r_{\chi}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{C^{\alpha_{1}}(\overline{B_{L_{0}}})}\big(|B_{j,\pm}k(\rho)|+\|r_{\sigma}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{C^{\alpha_{1}}(\overline{B_{L_{0}}})}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(1+\rho^{2+\theta_{2}}\big)=C\big(\rho^{\beta(\gamma_{2}+1)}+1\big)\qquad\,\text{for $\rho\geq\rho^{*}$},

with
L_{0}:=|\operatorname{supp}\psi|+2
and
\beta=\frac{\theta_{2}+2}{\gamma_{2}+1}\in(0,1)
.
Thus, using Lemmas
8.2
and
8.7
,
and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\rightarrow 0}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\langle\,\nu_{(t,r)},\,(P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau}){\mathbf{I}}_{\{\rho\geq\rho^{*}\}}\rangle\,\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle=Y(\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\sum_{\pm}(K^{\pm})^{2}\langle\nu_{(t,r)},\,Z(\rho)\,\overline{\chi(u\pm k(\rho))}\,\psi(u\pm k(\rho)){\mathbf{I}}_{\{\rho\geq\rho^{*}\}}\rangle,

which, with (
8.13
), yields (
8.8
). This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

Lemma 8.9
.

For any test function
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\tau\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\Big(\overline{P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}}\;\,\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1})-\chi(s_{1})\,P_{2}\sigma_{2}^{\tau}}

\displaystyle\quad-\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}}\;\,\overline{P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1})+\chi(s_{1})\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}}\Big)\,\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}=0.

(8.16)

Proof.
Fix
(\rho,u)\in\mathbb{H}
. It follows from Lemma
8.7
that

\big(\chi(s_{1})\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1})\big)(\rho,u,s_{1})\longrightarrow X(\rho,u,s_{1})\qquad\text{uniformly in }s_{1}\text{ as }\tau\to 0.

It is clear that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}}\,\big(\chi(s_{1})\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1})\big)\,\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{H}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau})(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u},s_{1})\,\big(\chi(s_{1})\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1})\big)(\rho,u,s_{1})\,\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u}).

It follows from Lemma
4.14
that
P_{j}\chi_{j}^{\tau},j=2,3
, are measures in
s_{1}
such that

\|P_{j}\chi_{j}^{\tau}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u},\cdot)\|_{\mathfrak{M},\alpha}\leq C_{\alpha}\tilde{\rho}\qquad\,\,\mbox{for large $\tilde{\rho}$},

where

\|\mu\|_{\mathfrak{M},\alpha}=\sup\left\{|\langle\mu,f\rangle|\,:\,f\in C_{0}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})\right.

and
\left.\|f\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}\leq 1\right\}
with
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
Then we use Lemma
8.2
and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem
to pass the limit inside the Young measure to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}}\,\big(\chi(s_{1})\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1})\big)\,\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\int_{\mathcal{H}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{1}\chi)(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u},s_{1})X(\rho,u,s_{1})\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u})

pointwise in
(\rho,u)
as
\tau\to 0
.
Now we are going to prove

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}}\,\big(\chi(s_{1})\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1})\big)\,\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\Big|\leq C\big(\rho^{\beta(\gamma_{2}+1)}+1\big)

(8.17)

for some constants
C>0
and
\beta\in(0,1]
, which are both independent of
\tau
.
Once (
8.17
) is proved, it follows from Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\tau\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}}(s_{1})\,\overline{\chi(s_{1})\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1}))}\,\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{\tau\to 0}\int_{\mathcal{H}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{P_{2}\chi_{2}^{\tau}}(s_{1})\,\big(\chi(s_{1})\,P_{3}\sigma_{3}^{\tau}-P_{3}\chi_{3}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1})\big)(\rho,u,s_{1})\,\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{H}}\int_{\mathcal{H}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{1}\chi)(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u},s_{1})X(\rho,u,s_{1})\psi(s_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{(t,r)}(\rho,u).

Since
X(\rho,u,s_{1})
is independent of the choice of the mollifying functions
\phi_{2}^{\tau}
and
\phi_{3}^{\tau}
from Lemma
8.7
, we may
interchange the roles of
s_{2}
and
s_{3}
to conclude the proof of (
8.9
).

To see the validity of (
8.17
), we begin by observing that, for
j=2,3
,

\overline{P_{j}\chi_{j}^{\tau}}(s_{1})
and
\psi(s_{1})
are independent of
(\rho,u)
.
Then it suffices to estimate the function:

\chi(s_{1})\,P_{j}\sigma_{j}^{\tau}-P_{j}\chi_{j}^{\tau}\,\sigma(s_{1})=\chi(s_{1})\,P_{j}(\sigma_{j}^{\tau}-u\chi_{j}^{\tau})-(\sigma(s_{1})-u\chi(s_{1}))\,P_{j}\chi_{j}^{\tau}.

(8.18)

It follows from Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
and
4.14
(also see
(
Chen-LeFloch-2000
, Proof of Lemma 4.2)
) that

\mbox{RHS of }\eqref{7.99}=E^{1,\tau}+E^{2,\tau}+E^{3,\tau}+E^{4,\tau},

with

\displaystyle E^{1,\tau}=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\pm}\big(A_{1,\pm}(\rho)b_{1}(\rho)G_{\lambda_{1}}(s_{1})+A_{1,\pm}(\rho)b_{2}(\rho)G_{\lambda_{1}+1}(s_{1})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\big((s_{j}-s_{1})\delta(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{\pm}\big(A_{3,\pm}(\rho)b_{1}(\rho)G_{\lambda_{1}}(s_{1})+A_{3,\pm}(\rho)b_{2}(\rho)G_{\lambda_{1}+1}(s_{1})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\big((s_{j}-s_{1})PV(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle E_{1}^{1,\tau}+E_{2}^{1,\tau},

\displaystyle E^{2,\tau}=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\pm}A_{1,\pm}(\rho)g_{2}(s_{1})\big(\delta(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))*\phi_{j}^{\tau}\big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\pm}A_{3,\pm}(\rho)g_{2}(s_{1})\big(PV(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))*\phi_{j}^{\tau}\big)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle E_{1}^{2,\tau}+E_{2}^{2,\tau},

\displaystyle E^{3,\tau}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\big(B_{1,\pm}(\rho)a_{1}(\rho)-A_{1,\pm}(\rho)b_{1}(\rho)\big)G_{\lambda_{1}}(s_{1})\big((s_{j}-u)\delta(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}\big(B_{1,\pm}(\rho)a_{2}(\rho)-A_{1,\pm}(\rho)b_{2}(\rho)\big)G_{\lambda_{1}+1}(s_{1})\big((s_{j}-u)\delta(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}B_{1,\pm}(\rho)g_{1}(s_{1})\big((s_{j}-u)\delta(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}\big(B_{3,\pm}(\rho)a_{1}(\rho)-A_{3,\pm}(\rho)b_{1}(\rho)\big)G_{\lambda_{1}}(s_{1})\big((s_{j}-u)PV(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}\big(B_{3,\pm}(\rho)a_{2}(\rho)-A_{3,\pm}(\rho)b_{2}(\rho)\big)G_{\lambda_{1}+1}(s_{1})\big((s_{j}-u)PV(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}B_{3,\pm}(\rho)g_{1}(s_{1})\big((s_{j}-u)PV(s_{j}-u\pm k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle E_{1}^{3,\tau}+E_{2}^{3,\tau}+E_{3}^{3,\tau}+E_{4}^{3,\tau}+E_{5}^{3,\tau}+E_{6}^{3,\tau},

and
E^{4,\tau}
is the remainder term which consists of the mollification of continuous functions,
where we have used the notation:
G_{\lambda_{1}}(s_{1})=[k(\rho)^{2}-(u-s_{1})^{2}]^{\lambda_{1}}
,
and
g_{i}(s_{1})=g_{i}(\rho,u-s_{1})
for
i=1,2
.

We first demonstrate the uniform bound on the term involving the delta measures. By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\delta(s_{j}-u+k(\rho))*\phi_{j}^{\tau}=\frac{1}{\tau}\phi_{j}(\frac{s_{1}-u+k(\rho)}{\tau}),

\displaystyle\big((s_{j}-s_{1})\delta(s_{j}-u+k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}=-\frac{s_{1}-u+k(\rho)}{\tau}\phi_{j}(\frac{s_{1}-u+k(\rho)}{\tau}),

\displaystyle\big((s_{j}-u)\delta(s_{j}-u+k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle\,\,\,=\big((s_{j}-s_{1})\delta(s_{j}-u+k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}+\big((s_{1}-u)\delta(s_{j}-u+k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle\,\,\,=(s_{1}-u)\tau^{-1}\phi_{j}(\frac{s_{1}-u+k(\rho)}{\tau})-\frac{s_{1}-u+k(\rho)}{\tau}\phi_{j}(\frac{s_{1}-u+k(\rho)}{\tau}).

(8.19)

Noting that

\begin{split}&G_{\lambda_{1}+1}(s_{1})=G_{\lambda_{1}}(s_{1})\,(k(\rho)-u+s_{1})\,(k(\rho)+u-s_{1}),\\[2.84526pt]
&|g_{i}(s_{1})|\leq\|\partial_{u}g_{i}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}|s_{1}-u\pm k(\rho)|\qquad\mbox{ for $i=1,2$},\end{split}

(8.20)

using (
8
)–(
8.20
)
and Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
and
4.11
–
4.14
, we obtain

E_{1}^{3,\tau}=0,\qquad|E_{1}^{1,\tau}|+|E_{1}^{2,\tau}|+|E_{2}^{3,\tau}|+|E_{3}^{3,\tau}|\leq C_{\phi}\big(1+\rho^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\big).

(8.21)

For the term involving with the principal value distribution, a direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle|PV*\phi_{j}^{\tau}(x)|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\tau}(x-y)-\phi_{j}^{\tau}(x+y)}{y}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{y}\big(\phi_{j}(\frac{x+y}{\tau})-\phi_{j}(\frac{x-y}{\tau})\big)\,\mathrm{d}y\Big|.

If
|x|\leq 2\tau
, we have

\displaystyle|PV*\phi_{j}^{\tau}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{0}^{4\tau}\frac{1}{|y|}\big|\phi_{j}(\frac{x-y}{\tau})-\phi_{j}(\frac{y+x}{\tau})\big|\,\mathrm{d}y\leq C\frac{1}{\tau}\|\phi_{j}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}=\frac{C_{\phi}}{|x|}.

(8.22)

On the other hand, if
|x|\geq 2\tau
, we can obtain

|PV*\phi_{j}^{\tau}(x)|\leq\frac{2}{\tau}\int_{|x|-\tau}^{|x|+\tau}\frac{\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|x|-\tau}\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{C_{\phi}}{|x|}.

(8.23)

Notice that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\big((s_{j}-s_{1})PV(s_{j}-u-k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

(8.24)

\displaystyle=\big((s_{j}-u+k(\rho))PV(s_{j}-u+k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle+\big((u-k(\rho)-s_{1})PV(s_{j}-u+k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau},

\displaystyle\big((s_{j}-u)PV(s_{j}-u-k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}

\displaystyle=\big((s_{j}-u+k(\rho))PV(s_{j}-u+k(\rho))\big)*\phi_{j}^{\tau}-k(\rho)PV(s_{j}-u+k(\rho))*\phi_{j}^{\tau},

which, with (
8.20
), (
8.22
)–(
8.24
), and
Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
and
4.11
–
4.14
, yields

\displaystyle E_{4}^{3,\tau}=0,\qquad|E_{2}^{1,\tau}|+|E_{2}^{2,\tau}|+|E_{5}^{3,\tau}|+|E_{6}^{3,\tau}|\leq C_{\phi}\big(1+\rho^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\big).

(8.25)

Combining (
8.21
) with (
8.25
) yields that there exists
\beta_{1}=\frac{3+\theta_{2}}{2(\gamma_{2}+1)}\in(0,1)
such that

|E^{1,\tau}+E^{2,\tau}+E^{3,\tau}|\leq C_{\phi}(1+\rho^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}})\leq C_{\phi}(1+\rho^{\beta_{1}(\gamma_{2}+1)}).

(8.26)

For
E^{4,\tau}
consisting of the mollification of continuous functions,
direct calculations show that

|E^{4,\tau}|\leq C_{\phi}\,\big(1+\rho^{2+\theta_{2}}|\ln\rho|\big)\leq C_{\phi}\,\big(1+\rho^{\beta_{2}(\gamma_{2}+1)}\big),

(8.27)

with
\beta_{2}=\frac{4+3\theta_{2}}{2(\gamma_{2}+1)}\in(0,1)
.
Combining (
8.27
) with (
8.26
), we conclude the proof of (
8.17
).

\hfill\square

9
Existence of Global Finite-Energy Solutions of CEPEs

In this section, we complete the proof of Theorem
2.3
.
Since the proof is similar to
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
,
we sketch the proof for the self-containedness of this paper. We divide the proof into four steps.

1. Since
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})(t,r)
obtained in Theorem
2.1
satisfies all
the assumptions of Theorem
2.2
, then it follows from Theorem
2.2

that there exists a vector function
(\rho,m)(t,r)
such that, up to a subsequence as
\varepsilon\to 0
,

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow(\rho,m)\quad\text{{\it a.e.} $(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}$},

(9.1)

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow(\rho,m)\quad\text{in $L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})\times L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p_{2}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})$}

(9.2)

for
p_{1}\in[1,\gamma_{2}+1)
and
p_{2}\in[1,\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3})
, where
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p_{j}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})

represents
L^{p_{j}}([0,T]\times K)

for any
T>0
and

K\Subset(0,\infty),j=1,2
.

Noting (
9.1
) and
\rho^{\varepsilon}\geq 0

a.e.
from Lemma
6.1
,
it is direct to show that
\rho(t,r)\geq 0

a.e.
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
.
Moreover, it follows from (
2.22
) that
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}r=\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}r

is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))
.
Then using Fatou’s lemma yields

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|m(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho(t,r)}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)|^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t<\infty.

Thus,
m(t,r)=0

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho(t,r)=0\}
,
and we can define the limit velocity
u(t,r)
as

\displaystyle u(t,r)=\frac{m(t,r)}{\rho(t,r)}\qquad\text{{\it a.e.} on }\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho(t,r)\neq 0\},

\displaystyle u(t,r)=0\qquad\text{{\it a.e.} on }\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho(t,r)=0\text{ or }r=0\}.

Therefore,
m(t,r)=\rho(t,r)u(t,r)

a.e.
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
.
Also, we can define
\big(\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}\big)(t,r):=\sqrt{\rho(t,r)}u(t,r)
,
which is zero
a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho(t,r)=0\}
.
Moreover, using (
2.24
) and Fatou’s lemma, we obtain

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho|u|^{3}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{3}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)<\infty

for any

[d,D]\Subset(0,\infty)
.

By similar calculations as in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, §5)
, we obtain that, as
\varepsilon\to 0
,

\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\equiv\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}\equiv\sqrt{\rho}u\qquad\text{strongly in }L^{2}([0,T]\times[d,D];r^{n-1}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t)

(9.3)

for any
T>0
and

[d,D]\Subset(0,\infty)
.

From (
9.2
)–(
9.3
), we also obtain the convergence of the mechanical energy as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
:

\eta^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow\eta^{*}(\rho,m)\qquad\text{ in }L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}).

(9.4)

Using (
9.2
), (
9.4
), and Fatou’s lemma,
and taking limit
\varepsilon\to 0
in (
2.21
)–(
2.22
), we have

\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\eta^{*}(\rho,m)(t,r)+\rho^{\gamma_{2}}(t,r)+\rho(t,r)\big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t\leq C(M,E_{0})(t_{2}-t_{1}),

(9.5)

which implies

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\eta^{*}(\rho,m)(t,r)+\rho^{\gamma_{2}}(t,r)+\rho(t,r)\big)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\leq C(M,E_{0}).

(9.6)

This indicates that
\rho(t,r)\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\gamma_{2}}(\mathbb{R};r^{2}\mathrm{d}r))
,
which implies that
\rho(t,\mathbf{x})
is a function in
L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\gamma_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))

with
\gamma_{2}>1
(rather than a measure in
(t,\mathbf{x})
).
Therefore, no delta measure (
i.e.
, concentration) is formed in the density in the time
interval
[0,T]
, especially at the origin:
r=0
.

2. For the convergence of the gravitational potential functions
\Phi^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
,
by similar calculation in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, §5)
, we obtain that,
as
\varepsilon\to 0
(up to a subsequence),

\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}(t,r)r^{2}=\int_{0}^{r}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\longrightarrow\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\qquad\text{ {\it a.e.} $(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}$}.

(9.7)

Thus, using (
6.3
), (
9.1
), (
9.7
), Fatou’s lemma,
and similar arguments as in (
9.5
)–(
9.6
), we have

\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,y)\,y^{2}\mathrm{d}y\Big)\rho(t,r)\,r\mathrm{d}r\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\text{ for {\it a.e.} $\,t\geq 0$}.

On the other hand, it follows from (
6.4
) that there exists a function
\Phi(t,\mathbf{x})=\Phi(t,r)

such that, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
(up to a subsequence),

\displaystyle\Phi^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\Phi\qquad\text{ weak-star in $L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{\operatorname{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))$
and weakly in $L^{2}(0,T;H_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})$},

\displaystyle\|\Phi(t)\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla\Phi(t)\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)}\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\text{ {\it a.e.} $t\geq 0$}.

Thus, by (
9.7
) and the uniqueness of limit, we obtain
that
\Phi_{r}(t,r)r^{2}=\int_{0}^{r}\rho(t,z)z^{2}\,\mathrm{d}z

a.e.

(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
.
By
similar arguments in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, §5)
, we also have the strong convergence of the potential functions:

\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|(\Phi_{r}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{r})(t,r)\big|^{2}r^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}t=0\qquad\text{ for $\gamma_{2}>\frac{6}{5}$}.

(9.8)

3. Now we define

(\rho,\mathcal{M},\Phi)(t,\mathbf{x}):=(\rho(t,r),m(t,r)\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r},\Phi(t,r)).

Then it follows from (
2.20
), (
9.8
), and Fatou’s lemma that

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}+\rho e(\rho)-\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\Phi|^{2}\Big)(t,\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq(t_{2}-t_{1})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho_{0}e(\rho_{0})-\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x},

which implies that, for
a.e.

t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}+\rho e(\rho)-\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\Phi|^{2}\Big)(t,\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho_{0}e(\rho_{0})-\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\Phi_{0}|^{2}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}.

(9.9)

On the other hand, using (
2.22
), (
9.6
), and (
9.8
), we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}+\rho e(\rho)+\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\Phi|^{2}\Big)(t,\mathbf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\leq C(M,E_{0}).

(9.10)

Combining (
9
) with (
9.10
), we complete the proof of (
2.28
).

4. Using (
6.7
), (
6.9
)–(
6.10
), and
similar arguments as in
(
Chen-He-Wang-Yuan-2021
, §5)
,
we conclude the proof of (
2.29
)–(
2.31
)
which, along with Steps 1–3, shows that
(\rho,\mathcal{M},\Phi)(t,\mathbf{x})
is indeed a global weak solution
of problem (
1.1
) and (
1.13
)–(
1.14
) in sense of Definition
2.2
.
This completes the proof.

\hfill\square
